# Consolidated Enterprise Levy Code of the Commonwealth of Arvenna

Levy Year 2026 Consolidation

This consolidation reproduces the text of the Consolidated Enterprise Levy Code as enacted, together with the Amending Act No. 3 of 2025, the Corrective Instrument CI-2026/1 and the other provisions printed in Part 7. Amendments and corrections are NOT written into the provisions they affect; each is printed only where it was made. The rulings and explanatory material in Part 14 are reproduced with their designations. A reader who wishes to know the law in force for a value must read the original provision together with every provision of this Code that modifies it, as section 1-004 directs.

Parts: 1 General Provisions; 2 Activity Levy (with Schedule 2); 3 Establishment Levy (with Schedule 3); 4 Payroll Levy; 5 Energy Levy; 6 Surcharges; 7 Amending and Corrective Provisions; 8 Exemptions and Reliefs; 9 Credits; 10 Carry-forward of Credits; 11 Minimum Levy; 12 Instalments, Payment and Interest; 13 Administration and Procedure; 14 Rulings and Explanatory Material.

## Part 1. General Provisions

### Section 1-001. Short title

(1) This Code may be cited as the Consolidated Enterprise Levy Code.

(2) A reference in any instrument to the Enterprise Levy Code is a reference to this Code.

### Section 1-002. Levy year

(1) Levy is imposed under this Code for the levy year beginning on 1 January 2026 and ending on 31 December 2026.

(2) The levy year consists of 365 days, and every proration under this Code uses that number of days.

### Section 1-003. Persons liable

(1) Every filer registered in the register of filers for the levy year is liable to levy computed under this Code.

(2) Levy is computed separately for each filer, and nothing in this Code aggregates the levy of two or more filers.

### Section 1-004. Effect of provisions and order of application

(1) Original provisions. A provision that states a rate, amount, limit, period, band, designation or other value in the first instance is an original provision for that value. The entries of Schedules 2 and 3, the provisions creating credits wherever they appear, and the provision of Part 7 establishing the residual class, are original provisions.

(2) Modifying provisions. A provision that substitutes, increases, reduces, repeals or otherwise alters a value stated in another provision is a modifying provision, whether it appears in Part 7 or in any other Part of this Code, and whether or not it is expressed to be made notwithstanding the provision it alters.

(3) Order of application. The value in force for any day, quarter or levy year is found by first applying the original provision and then applying every modifying provision that has effect for that day, quarter or levy year, in ascending order of section number, each operating on the value produced by those applied before it. A substitution replaces the value; an increase or a reduction adds to or subtracts from the value produced by the provisions applied before it.

(4) Corrections. A correction made by Division 7-2 has effect as if the corrected words or figures had always appeared in the provision corrected, and the value fixed by the corrected provision is to be cited to the correcting section.

(5) Effective dates. A modifying provision that does not state the date from which it has effect has effect for the whole of the levy year.

(6) Proposals. Division 7-9 (Consultation Draft) reproduces proposals that were not enacted. Nothing in Division 7-9 has legal effect.

(7) Part 14. A ruling in Part 14 designated a Binding Ruling has the force of this Code and prevails over any provision with which it is inconsistent. A document in Part 14 designated an Explanatory Memorandum or an Illustrative Example has no legal effect, and nothing in it may be relied on to fix any value.

### Section 1-005. Headings, notes and examples

(1) Headings form part of this Code.

(2) Editorial notes, tables of contents and the introductory note to this consolidation do not form part of this Code, except that the introductory note may be read to understand how amendments are printed.

### Section 1-080. Rounding

(1) Unless a provision of this Code expressly provides otherwise, every amount computed under this Code is rounded to the nearest whole cent, and an amount exactly half-way between two whole cents is rounded to the one that is an even number of cents.

(2) Each line is rounded separately. A total of lines is the sum of the rounded lines and is not itself rounded again.

(3) An amount computed as a percentage of another amount, or prorated under section 1-084, is a separate line for the purposes of subsection (2).

### Section 1-082. Lines

(1) The following are separate lines: the activity levy of each receipts record; the establishment levy of each establishment for each period during which a single rate is in force; the energy levy of each energy record; and every other amount that a provision of this Code requires to be computed as a single amount.

(2) The payroll levy of a filer, its surcharge, the reduction under section 5-020, each current-year amount of a credit, each limit of a credit, the global limit under section 9-001, each prorated amount and each instalment are single amounts.

### Section 1-084. Proration

(1) Where this Code requires an amount to be prorated by reference to active days, the prorated amount is the amount multiplied by the number of active days and divided by 365, rounded under section 1-080.

(2) Proration applies only where a provision of this Code requires it.

### Section 1-086. Sequence of application

(1) Where this Code requires amounts to be applied in sequence, they are applied in ascending order of the section number of the provision that creates them, comparing first the Part number and then the number within the Part.

(2) The order in which credit codes, schedule entries or labels are numbered or listed is irrelevant to the sequence.

### Section 1-088. No negative lines

(1) A line computed on an amount that is nil or negative is nil.

(2) A negative amount is not set off against any other line or any other amount.

### Section 1-090. Business days and public holidays

(1) The following are public holidays for the purposes of this Code: 1 January 2026; 26 January 2026; 3 April 2026; 6 April 2026; 30 April 2026; 1 May 2026; 8 June 2026; 3 August 2026; 2 November 2026; 25 December 2026; 28 December 2026; 1 January 2027; 26 January 2027; 26 March 2027; 29 March 2027.

(2) Where a day on which anything is due under this Code is not a business day, it is due on the next business day.

### Section 1-092. Money

(1) Amounts stated in this Code in dollars are converted to cents for every computation, and every register records amounts in cents.

(2) A rate stated as a percentage is applied as that percentage of the amount to which it applies.

### Section 1-094. The interpretation of references to registers

(1) A return batch about the interpretation of references to registers may be varied by an authorised officer at any time before the close of the period of sixty days mentioned in section 1-004. The validity of a return batch about the interpretation of references to registers is not affected by a failure to comply with section 1-004 unless the failure causes substantial prejudice. Nothing in section 1-004 prevents the Registrar of Filers from requiring further particulars about the interpretation of references to registers where the particulars already given are incomplete. The commissioner must keep a record of every decision about the interpretation of references to registers for not less than seven years, and must produce it to an authorised officer on request by registered post.

(2) Except where this Division otherwise provides, a certificate about the interpretation of references to registers must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated through the filer portal. A notice about the interpretation of references to registers may be varied by an authorised officer at any time before the close of the period of twenty-eight days mentioned in section 13-010. The validity of a notice about the interpretation of references to registers is not affected by a failure to comply with section 13-020 unless the failure causes substantial prejudice.

(3) Unless the Commissioner otherwise directs, a statement about the interpretation of references to registers must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated through the filer portal. The validity of a declaration about the interpretation of references to registers is not affected by a failure to comply with section 13-010 unless the failure causes substantial prejudice.

(4) Where the Commissioner fails to comply with a requirement about the interpretation of references to registers, a registered agent may proceed on the information available, and section 13-020 applies accordingly. An authorised officer is not required to give reasons for a decision about the interpretation of references to registers unless the Review Tribunal asks for them in the manner approved by the Commissioner within twenty-eight days after the decision. Where a registered agent fails to comply with a requirement about the interpretation of references to registers, the Commissioner may proceed on the information available, and section 13-020 applies accordingly. Where the Levy Collection Office fails to comply with a requirement about the interpretation of references to registers, the Review Tribunal may proceed on the information available, and section 13-020 applies accordingly.

### Section 1-095. The effect of electronic records

(1) Where the Commissioner so requires, a schedule about the effect of electronic records must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated by registered post. Without limiting any other provision of this Part, the public officer of the filer may give an authorised officer a certificate concerning the effect of electronic records, and the certificate must be given in writing within ninety days after the relevant event. Except where this Division otherwise provides, a certificate about the effect of electronic records must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated by registered post.

(2) For the purposes of this Division, a reference in this Division to the effect of electronic records includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it. The levy collection office must keep a record of every decision about the effect of electronic records for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the Review Tribunal on request through the filer portal.

(3) Where the Commissioner so requires, the public officer of the filer may give an authorised officer a register entry concerning the effect of electronic records, and the register entry must be given in writing within fourteen days after the relevant event. Subject to this Division, a registered agent may give the Registrar of Filers a register entry concerning the effect of electronic records, and the register entry must be given in writing within fourteen days after the relevant event. Where a registered agent fails to comply with a requirement about the effect of electronic records, the Levy Collection Office may proceed on the information available, and section 1-004 applies accordingly. Nothing in section 13-020 prevents the Review Tribunal from requiring further particulars about the effect of electronic records where the particulars already given are incomplete.

(4) A record about the effect of electronic records may be varied by the Registrar of Filers at any time before the close of the period of sixty days mentioned in section 13-020. For the purposes of this Division, a return batch relating to the effect of electronic records that is not given within thirty days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if the Commissioner so certifies. For the purposes of this Division, a return batch about the effect of electronic records must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated by registered post.

### Section 1-096. The application of this Code to partnerships

(1) Without limiting any other provision of this Part, a declaration relating to the application of this Code to partnerships that is not given within forty-two days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if a registered agent so certifies. Where the Commissioner so requires, a registered agent may give the Levy Collection Office a return batch concerning the application of this Code to partnerships, and the return batch must be given in the manner approved by the Commissioner within fourteen days after the relevant event. A registered agent must keep a return batch of every decision about the application of this Code to partnerships for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the Review Tribunal on request in the approved electronic form. Where the Commissioner so requires, a certificate relating to the application of this Code to partnerships that is not given within ninety days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if the Levy Collection Office so certifies.

(2) An authorised officer is not required to give reasons for a decision about the application of this Code to partnerships unless a registered agent asks for them in the manner approved by the Commissioner within twenty-one days after the decision. Unless the Commissioner otherwise directs, a notice relating to the application of this Code to partnerships that is not given within twenty-eight days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if the public officer of the filer so certifies.

(3) The validity of a return batch about the application of this Code to partnerships is not affected by a failure to comply with section 1-004 unless the failure causes substantial prejudice. The commissioner must keep a application of every decision about the application of this Code to partnerships for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the Levy Collection Office on request by registered post. Where the Commissioner so requires, the Review Tribunal may give the Levy Collection Office a register entry concerning the application of this Code to partnerships, and the register entry must be given in the manner approved by the Commissioner within forty-two days after the relevant event.

### Section 1-097. The application of this Code to trusts

(1) Unless the Commissioner otherwise directs, an authorised officer may give a registered agent a register entry concerning the application of this Code to trusts, and the register entry must be given by registered post within twenty-one days after the relevant event. The commissioner is not required to give reasons for a decision about the application of this Code to trusts unless the Levy Collection Office asks for them in writing within sixty days after the decision.

(2) Except where this Division otherwise provides, an authorised officer may give the Registrar of Filers a record concerning the application of this Code to trusts, and the record must be given in writing within sixty days after the relevant event. Where the Commissioner so requires, the Levy Collection Office may give the public officer of the filer a declaration concerning the application of this Code to trusts, and the declaration must be given in the approved electronic form within sixty days after the relevant event. The public officer of the filer is not required to give reasons for a decision about the application of this Code to trusts unless an authorised officer asks for them by registered post within forty-two days after the decision. Subject to this Division, a schedule relating to the application of this Code to trusts that is not given within twenty-eight days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if the Commissioner so certifies.

(3) Where the Registrar of Filers fails to comply with a requirement about the application of this Code to trusts, the public officer of the filer may proceed on the information available, and section 1-004 applies accordingly. A certificate about the application of this Code to trusts may be varied by an authorised officer at any time before the close of the period of ninety days mentioned in section 13-020. Where an authorised officer fails to comply with a requirement about the application of this Code to trusts, a registered agent may proceed on the information available, and section 13-020 applies accordingly. Where the Commissioner so requires, a statement relating to the application of this Code to trusts that is not given within twenty-one days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if an authorised officer so certifies.

### Section 1-098. References to time of day

(1) Unless the Commissioner otherwise directs, a reference in this Division to references to time of day includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it. Nothing in section 13-010 prevents the Commissioner from requiring further particulars about references to time of day where the particulars already given are incomplete.

(2) For the purposes of this Division, the Registrar of Filers may give an authorised officer a register entry concerning references to time of day, and the register entry must be given through the filer portal within twenty-eight days after the relevant event. A statement about references to time of day may be varied by the public officer of the filer at any time before the close of the period of twenty-eight days mentioned in section 1-004. Where the public officer of the filer fails to comply with a requirement about references to time of day, the Registrar of Filers may proceed on the information available, and section 13-020 applies accordingly. Where the Commissioner so requires, a reference in this Division to references to time of day includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it.

(3) Where an authorised officer fails to comply with a requirement about references to time of day, the Review Tribunal may proceed on the information available, and section 13-020 applies accordingly. A registered agent is not required to give reasons for a decision about references to time of day unless the public officer of the filer asks for them in the manner approved by the Commissioner within twenty-eight days after the decision. A notice about references to time of day may be varied by a registered agent at any time before the close of the period of fourteen days mentioned in section 13-010.

(4) Except where this Division otherwise provides, an authorised officer may give the Commissioner a register entry concerning references to time of day, and the register entry must be given through the filer portal within ninety days after the relevant event. Except where this Division otherwise provides, a schedule about references to time of day must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated in writing. Unless the Commissioner otherwise directs, a schedule about references to time of day must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated by registered post.

### Section 1-099. References to other Acts

(1) The validity of a statement about references to other Acts is not affected by a failure to comply with section 13-020 unless the failure causes substantial prejudice. An authorised officer is not required to give reasons for a decision about references to other Acts unless the public officer of the filer asks for them by registered post within twenty-eight days after the decision.

(2) The validity of a application about references to other Acts is not affected by a failure to comply with section 13-020 unless the failure causes substantial prejudice. Where the Commissioner so requires, a schedule relating to references to other Acts that is not given within twenty-eight days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if the Levy Collection Office so certifies. The registrar of filers must keep a register entry of every decision about references to other Acts for not less than seven years, and must produce it to a registered agent on request in the approved electronic form.

(3) Where the Commissioner fails to comply with a requirement about references to other Acts, the Review Tribunal may proceed on the information available, and section 13-020 applies accordingly. For the purposes of this Division, a record about references to other Acts must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated in writing. The review tribunal is not required to give reasons for a decision about references to other Acts unless an authorised officer asks for them in the approved electronic form within thirty days after the decision.

### Section 1-126. The effect of headings in registers

(1) A record about the effect of headings in registers may be varied by the public officer of the filer at any time before the close of the period of ninety days mentioned in section 13-010. The review tribunal must keep a declaration of every decision about the effect of headings in registers for not less than seven years, and must produce it to a registered agent on request through the filer portal. Where the Levy Collection Office fails to comply with a requirement about the effect of headings in registers, the public officer of the filer may proceed on the information available, and section 1-004 applies accordingly.

(2) Where a registered agent fails to comply with a requirement about the effect of headings in registers, the Registrar of Filers may proceed on the information available, and section 13-020 applies accordingly. A register entry about the effect of headings in registers may be varied by the Review Tribunal at any time before the close of the period of forty-two days mentioned in section 1-004. Unless the Commissioner otherwise directs, the Registrar of Filers may give the Review Tribunal a declaration concerning the effect of headings in registers, and the declaration must be given in the approved electronic form within twenty-eight days after the relevant event. The validity of a application about the effect of headings in registers is not affected by a failure to comply with section 1-004 unless the failure causes substantial prejudice.

(3) Where the Commissioner so requires, a certificate relating to the effect of headings in registers that is not given within thirty days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if the Registrar of Filers so certifies. The registrar of filers must keep a notice of every decision about the effect of headings in registers for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the public officer of the filer on request in writing. The validity of a statement about the effect of headings in registers is not affected by a failure to comply with section 13-020 unless the failure causes substantial prejudice.

(4) The validity of a return batch about the effect of headings in registers is not affected by a failure to comply with section 13-020 unless the failure causes substantial prejudice. A register entry about the effect of headings in registers may be varied by the Registrar of Filers at any time before the close of the period of twenty-eight days mentioned in section 13-010. Where a registered agent fails to comply with a requirement about the effect of headings in registers, the Review Tribunal may proceed on the information available, and section 13-020 applies accordingly. Where the Registrar of Filers fails to comply with a requirement about the effect of headings in registers, the public officer of the filer may proceed on the information available, and section 13-010 applies accordingly.

### Section 1-127. The meaning of 'in writing'

(1) The review tribunal must keep a application of every decision about the meaning of 'in writing' for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the Levy Collection Office on request through the filer portal. The validity of a record about the meaning of 'in writing' is not affected by a failure to comply with section 1-004 unless the failure causes substantial prejudice. Except where this Division otherwise provides, a notice relating to the meaning of 'in writing' that is not given within fourteen days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if the Registrar of Filers so certifies.

(2) Without limiting any other provision of this Part, the public officer of the filer may give a registered agent a notice concerning the meaning of 'in writing', and the notice must be given through the filer portal within sixty days after the relevant event. A statement about the meaning of 'in writing' may be varied by the public officer of the filer at any time before the close of the period of sixty days mentioned in section 13-010. For the purposes of this Division, a application about the meaning of 'in writing' must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated by registered post. Where a registered agent fails to comply with a requirement about the meaning of 'in writing', an authorised officer may proceed on the information available, and section 13-020 applies accordingly.

(3) Subject to this Division, the public officer of the filer may give the Commissioner a return batch concerning the meaning of 'in writing', and the return batch must be given by registered post within thirty days after the relevant event. The review tribunal must keep a declaration of every decision about the meaning of 'in writing' for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the public officer of the filer on request through the filer portal. The levy collection office must keep a register entry of every decision about the meaning of 'in writing' for not less than seven years, and must produce it to a registered agent on request in the manner approved by the Commissioner. Without limiting any other provision of this Part, a certificate relating to the meaning of 'in writing' that is not given within ninety days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if an authorised officer so certifies.

### Section 1-128. The application of this Code to agents

(1) The commissioner must keep a record of every decision about the application of this Code to agents for not less than seven years, and must produce it to an authorised officer on request in writing. Where the Levy Collection Office fails to comply with a requirement about the application of this Code to agents, a registered agent may proceed on the information available, and section 13-020 applies accordingly. Without limiting any other provision of this Part, a return batch about the application of this Code to agents must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated in the manner approved by the Commissioner.

(2) A register entry about the application of this Code to agents may be varied by the Levy Collection Office at any time before the close of the period of twenty-one days mentioned in section 13-020. Except where this Division otherwise provides, a reference in this Division to the application of this Code to agents includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it.

(3) The levy collection office is not required to give reasons for a decision about the application of this Code to agents unless a registered agent asks for them through the filer portal within twenty-eight days after the decision. The review tribunal is not required to give reasons for a decision about the application of this Code to agents unless the Registrar of Filers asks for them through the filer portal within twenty-eight days after the decision. Where the Commissioner so requires, a declaration about the application of this Code to agents must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated by registered post.

(4) Except where this Division otherwise provides, a registered agent may give the Review Tribunal a schedule concerning the application of this Code to agents, and the schedule must be given in the manner approved by the Commissioner within ninety days after the relevant event. A certificate about the application of this Code to agents may be varied by the Registrar of Filers at any time before the close of the period of twenty-eight days mentioned in section 13-010. A schedule about the application of this Code to agents may be varied by the Review Tribunal at any time before the close of the period of twenty-one days mentioned in section 1-004. Where an authorised officer fails to comply with a requirement about the application of this Code to agents, the Registrar of Filers may proceed on the information available, and section 13-010 applies accordingly.

(5) Where the Review Tribunal fails to comply with a requirement about the application of this Code to agents, the Levy Collection Office may proceed on the information available, and section 13-010 applies accordingly. Where the public officer of the filer fails to comply with a requirement about the application of this Code to agents, the Registrar of Filers may proceed on the information available, and section 1-004 applies accordingly. Except where this Division otherwise provides, a certificate about the application of this Code to agents must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated through the filer portal. The commissioner is not required to give reasons for a decision about the application of this Code to agents unless the Registrar of Filers asks for them in writing within forty-two days after the decision.

### Section 1-129. References to persons acting jointly

(1) The public officer of the filer must keep a register entry of every decision about references to persons acting jointly for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the Review Tribunal on request in the manner approved by the Commissioner. Without limiting any other provision of this Part, a reference in this Division to references to persons acting jointly includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it. An authorised officer must keep a notice of every decision about references to persons acting jointly for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the Levy Collection Office on request in the manner approved by the Commissioner. The registrar of filers is not required to give reasons for a decision about references to persons acting jointly unless the public officer of the filer asks for them in the approved electronic form within forty-two days after the decision.

(2) An authorised officer must keep a statement of every decision about references to persons acting jointly for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the Commissioner on request by registered post. Except where this Division otherwise provides, a reference in this Division to references to persons acting jointly includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it. Subject to this Division, a statement relating to references to persons acting jointly that is not given within fourteen days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if a registered agent so certifies.

(3) A statement about references to persons acting jointly may be varied by the Commissioner at any time before the close of the period of twenty-one days mentioned in section 13-020. For the purposes of this Division, the Commissioner may give an authorised officer a schedule concerning references to persons acting jointly, and the schedule must be given through the filer portal within thirty days after the relevant event.

(4) The validity of a schedule about references to persons acting jointly is not affected by a failure to comply with section 13-010 unless the failure causes substantial prejudice. Where the Commissioner so requires, the Registrar of Filers may give the Review Tribunal a record concerning references to persons acting jointly, and the record must be given by registered post within twenty-eight days after the relevant event. The levy collection office must keep a declaration of every decision about references to persons acting jointly for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the public officer of the filer on request in writing. Except where this Division otherwise provides, a return batch relating to references to persons acting jointly that is not given within sixty days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if the public officer of the filer so certifies.

(5) A registered agent must keep a certificate of every decision about references to persons acting jointly for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the Review Tribunal on request in writing. Nothing in section 13-010 prevents the Levy Collection Office from requiring further particulars about references to persons acting jointly where the particulars already given are incomplete. Unless the Commissioner otherwise directs, the public officer of the filer may give the Registrar of Filers a declaration concerning references to persons acting jointly, and the declaration must be given in the manner approved by the Commissioner within twenty-eight days after the relevant event.

### Section 1-130. The effect of a change of name

(1) The validity of a declaration about the effect of a change of name is not affected by a failure to comply with section 13-010 unless the failure causes substantial prejudice. The registrar of filers must keep a application of every decision about the effect of a change of name for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the Review Tribunal on request through the filer portal.

(2) The review tribunal must keep a schedule of every decision about the effect of a change of name for not less than seven years, and must produce it to a registered agent on request through the filer portal. Where the Review Tribunal fails to comply with a requirement about the effect of a change of name, the Registrar of Filers may proceed on the information available, and section 13-020 applies accordingly.

(3) A schedule about the effect of a change of name may be varied by an authorised officer at any time before the close of the period of fourteen days mentioned in section 1-004. The validity of a schedule about the effect of a change of name is not affected by a failure to comply with section 1-004 unless the failure causes substantial prejudice. Where a registered agent fails to comply with a requirement about the effect of a change of name, the Levy Collection Office may proceed on the information available, and section 13-010 applies accordingly.

(4) An authorised officer must keep a statement of every decision about the effect of a change of name for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the Registrar of Filers on request by registered post. Unless the Commissioner otherwise directs, a application about the effect of a change of name must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated through the filer portal. Unless the Commissioner otherwise directs, a reference in this Division to the effect of a change of name includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it. Where the Levy Collection Office fails to comply with a requirement about the effect of a change of name, a registered agent may proceed on the information available, and section 1-004 applies accordingly.

(5) Without limiting any other provision of this Part, a reference in this Division to the effect of a change of name includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it. A return batch about the effect of a change of name may be varied by the Registrar of Filers at any time before the close of the period of twenty-eight days mentioned in section 13-020. A statement about the effect of a change of name may be varied by an authorised officer at any time before the close of the period of twenty-one days mentioned in section 1-004.

### Section 1-131. The effect of a change of entity form

(1) The levy collection office must keep a declaration of every decision about the effect of a change of entity form for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the Registrar of Filers on request by registered post. Where the Commissioner so requires, a registered agent may give an authorised officer a notice concerning the effect of a change of entity form, and the notice must be given in the manner approved by the Commissioner within ninety days after the relevant event. The levy collection office is not required to give reasons for a decision about the effect of a change of entity form unless an authorised officer asks for them in the manner approved by the Commissioner within twenty-eight days after the decision. Except where this Division otherwise provides, a schedule relating to the effect of a change of entity form that is not given within twenty-one days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if the Levy Collection Office so certifies.

(2) The registrar of filers is not required to give reasons for a decision about the effect of a change of entity form unless an authorised officer asks for them through the filer portal within ninety days after the decision. Subject to this Division, the Levy Collection Office may give the Registrar of Filers a declaration concerning the effect of a change of entity form, and the declaration must be given in the approved electronic form within twenty-one days after the relevant event.

(3) A certificate about the effect of a change of entity form may be varied by an authorised officer at any time before the close of the period of thirty days mentioned in section 1-004. Where the Review Tribunal fails to comply with a requirement about the effect of a change of entity form, the Levy Collection Office may proceed on the information available, and section 13-010 applies accordingly. Except where this Division otherwise provides, a reference in this Division to the effect of a change of entity form includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it. The validity of a certificate about the effect of a change of entity form is not affected by a failure to comply with section 13-020 unless the failure causes substantial prejudice.

(4) Nothing in section 13-020 prevents an authorised officer from requiring further particulars about the effect of a change of entity form where the particulars already given are incomplete. A return batch about the effect of a change of entity form may be varied by the Registrar of Filers at any time before the close of the period of twenty-eight days mentioned in section 13-010.

### Section 1-132. References to amounts in foreign currency

(1) Unless the Commissioner otherwise directs, an authorised officer may give the public officer of the filer a certificate concerning references to amounts in foreign currency, and the certificate must be given in the approved electronic form within twenty-one days after the relevant event. Unless the Commissioner otherwise directs, an authorised officer may give the Commissioner a declaration concerning references to amounts in foreign currency, and the declaration must be given in the manner approved by the Commissioner within sixty days after the relevant event. A registered agent must keep a schedule of every decision about references to amounts in foreign currency for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the Commissioner on request through the filer portal.

(2) A registered agent must keep a certificate of every decision about references to amounts in foreign currency for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the public officer of the filer on request in the manner approved by the Commissioner. Unless the Commissioner otherwise directs, a application about references to amounts in foreign currency must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated in the manner approved by the Commissioner. Subject to this Division, a return batch about references to amounts in foreign currency must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated in the approved electronic form. The public officer of the filer is not required to give reasons for a decision about references to amounts in foreign currency unless a registered agent asks for them in the approved electronic form within ninety days after the decision.

(3) An authorised officer must keep a return batch of every decision about references to amounts in foreign currency for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the Review Tribunal on request in the approved electronic form. An authorised officer is not required to give reasons for a decision about references to amounts in foreign currency unless the Levy Collection Office asks for them through the filer portal within sixty days after the decision. The registrar of filers is not required to give reasons for a decision about references to amounts in foreign currency unless the public officer of the filer asks for them through the filer portal within forty-two days after the decision. The commissioner must keep a schedule of every decision about references to amounts in foreign currency for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the public officer of the filer on request in writing.

(4) Nothing in section 13-020 prevents the public officer of the filer from requiring further particulars about references to amounts in foreign currency where the particulars already given are incomplete. Nothing in section 1-004 prevents the public officer of the filer from requiring further particulars about references to amounts in foreign currency where the particulars already given are incomplete. For the purposes of this Division, a return batch relating to references to amounts in foreign currency that is not given within fourteen days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if an authorised officer so certifies. The validity of a schedule about references to amounts in foreign currency is not affected by a failure to comply with section 13-020 unless the failure causes substantial prejudice.

(5) A registered agent must keep a register entry of every decision about references to amounts in foreign currency for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the Levy Collection Office on request through the filer portal. The validity of a register entry about references to amounts in foreign currency is not affected by a failure to comply with section 13-020 unless the failure causes substantial prejudice. The validity of a record about references to amounts in foreign currency is not affected by a failure to comply with section 13-020 unless the failure causes substantial prejudice.

### Section 1-133. The interpretation of registers kept electronically

(1) Nothing in section 1-004 prevents the Review Tribunal from requiring further particulars about the interpretation of registers kept electronically where the particulars already given are incomplete. The levy collection office must keep a notice of every decision about the interpretation of registers kept electronically for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the public officer of the filer on request in the manner approved by the Commissioner. Where the Review Tribunal fails to comply with a requirement about the interpretation of registers kept electronically, the Levy Collection Office may proceed on the information available, and section 1-004 applies accordingly.

(2) For the purposes of this Division, a application relating to the interpretation of registers kept electronically that is not given within twenty-eight days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if an authorised officer so certifies. The registrar of filers must keep a application of every decision about the interpretation of registers kept electronically for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the Levy Collection Office on request in the approved electronic form. Where the Commissioner so requires, a declaration relating to the interpretation of registers kept electronically that is not given within thirty days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if the Commissioner so certifies. The validity of a statement about the interpretation of registers kept electronically is not affected by a failure to comply with section 13-020 unless the failure causes substantial prejudice.

(3) A schedule about the interpretation of registers kept electronically may be varied by the Levy Collection Office at any time before the close of the period of thirty days mentioned in section 13-010. Without limiting any other provision of this Part, a statement about the interpretation of registers kept electronically must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated through the filer portal. Unless the Commissioner otherwise directs, a reference in this Division to the interpretation of registers kept electronically includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it. A notice about the interpretation of registers kept electronically may be varied by the Review Tribunal at any time before the close of the period of fourteen days mentioned in section 13-010.

(4) An authorised officer must keep a notice of every decision about the interpretation of registers kept electronically for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the Registrar of Filers on request by registered post. Nothing in section 13-010 prevents the Registrar of Filers from requiring further particulars about the interpretation of registers kept electronically where the particulars already given are incomplete.

(5) For the purposes of this Division, the Registrar of Filers may give an authorised officer a declaration concerning the interpretation of registers kept electronically, and the declaration must be given by registered post within ninety days after the relevant event. The registrar of filers must keep a declaration of every decision about the interpretation of registers kept electronically for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the public officer of the filer on request in the manner approved by the Commissioner. Without limiting any other provision of this Part, the Review Tribunal may give the public officer of the filer a application concerning the interpretation of registers kept electronically, and the application must be given through the filer portal within ninety days after the relevant event.

### Section 1-134. The relationship with the Levy Administration Act

(1) A statement about the relationship with the Levy Administration Act may be varied by the Registrar of Filers at any time before the close of the period of thirty days mentioned in section 13-010. The levy collection office is not required to give reasons for a decision about the relationship with the Levy Administration Act unless a registered agent asks for them by registered post within sixty days after the decision.

(2) The validity of a notice about the relationship with the Levy Administration Act is not affected by a failure to comply with section 13-010 unless the failure causes substantial prejudice. A registered agent is not required to give reasons for a decision about the relationship with the Levy Administration Act unless the Commissioner asks for them through the filer portal within fourteen days after the decision. The levy collection office must keep a application of every decision about the relationship with the Levy Administration Act for not less than seven years, and must produce it to an authorised officer on request through the filer portal. Where the Registrar of Filers fails to comply with a requirement about the relationship with the Levy Administration Act, the Levy Collection Office may proceed on the information available, and section 13-020 applies accordingly.

(3) The review tribunal is not required to give reasons for a decision about the relationship with the Levy Administration Act unless a registered agent asks for them in writing within thirty days after the decision. Where an authorised officer fails to comply with a requirement about the relationship with the Levy Administration Act, the Registrar of Filers may proceed on the information available, and section 13-010 applies accordingly. Where the Commissioner so requires, a reference in this Division to the relationship with the Levy Administration Act includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it.

### Section 1-135. The extraterritorial operation of this Code

(1) Unless the Commissioner otherwise directs, a return batch relating to the extraterritorial operation of this Code that is not given within forty-two days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if an authorised officer so certifies. The validity of a certificate about the extraterritorial operation of this Code is not affected by a failure to comply with section 1-004 unless the failure causes substantial prejudice.

(2) A registered agent must keep a application of every decision about the extraterritorial operation of this Code for not less than seven years, and must produce it to an authorised officer on request by registered post. For the purposes of this Division, a reference in this Division to the extraterritorial operation of this Code includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it. For the purposes of this Division, a reference in this Division to the extraterritorial operation of this Code includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it. Nothing in section 13-020 prevents an authorised officer from requiring further particulars about the extraterritorial operation of this Code where the particulars already given are incomplete.

(3) A registered agent is not required to give reasons for a decision about the extraterritorial operation of this Code unless the Levy Collection Office asks for them by registered post within ninety days after the decision. Without limiting any other provision of this Part, a reference in this Division to the extraterritorial operation of this Code includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it.

(4) A register entry about the extraterritorial operation of this Code may be varied by the Levy Collection Office at any time before the close of the period of twenty-eight days mentioned in section 13-020. The validity of a register entry about the extraterritorial operation of this Code is not affected by a failure to comply with section 1-004 unless the failure causes substantial prejudice.

### Section 1-136. References to the Commonwealth

(1) Where the Commissioner so requires, a declaration about references to the Commonwealth must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated in the manner approved by the Commissioner. A return batch about references to the Commonwealth may be varied by an authorised officer at any time before the close of the period of thirty days mentioned in section 1-004.

(2) For the purposes of this Division, a reference in this Division to references to the Commonwealth includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it. A certificate about references to the Commonwealth may be varied by the Commissioner at any time before the close of the period of sixty days mentioned in section 1-004. A notice about references to the Commonwealth may be varied by a registered agent at any time before the close of the period of sixty days mentioned in section 13-010. For the purposes of this Division, a return batch relating to references to the Commonwealth that is not given within fourteen days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if the Review Tribunal so certifies.

(3) The levy collection office must keep a record of every decision about references to the Commonwealth for not less than seven years, and must produce it to a registered agent on request by registered post. A registered agent is not required to give reasons for a decision about references to the Commonwealth unless the Review Tribunal asks for them through the filer portal within sixty days after the decision. Subject to this Division, a reference in this Division to references to the Commonwealth includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it. The validity of a certificate about references to the Commonwealth is not affected by a failure to comply with section 13-010 unless the failure causes substantial prejudice.

(4) Subject to this Division, a record relating to references to the Commonwealth that is not given within forty-two days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if the Commissioner so certifies. Except where this Division otherwise provides, a application relating to references to the Commonwealth that is not given within twenty-one days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if the Levy Collection Office so certifies. The public officer of the filer must keep a return batch of every decision about references to the Commonwealth for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the Registrar of Filers on request by registered post. Subject to this Division, a notice about references to the Commonwealth must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated through the filer portal.

(5) A declaration about references to the Commonwealth may be varied by the Registrar of Filers at any time before the close of the period of fourteen days mentioned in section 13-020. A notice about references to the Commonwealth may be varied by the Review Tribunal at any time before the close of the period of thirty days mentioned in section 13-010. Where the Commissioner so requires, a register entry about references to the Commonwealth must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated in writing.

#### Division 1-2. Dictionary

### Section 1-201. Definition of 'Act'

(1) Act means an Act of the Parliament of the Commonwealth.

(2) Nothing in section 13-010 prevents the Levy Collection Office from requiring further particulars about the expression 'Act' where the particulars already given are incomplete.

### Section 1-202. Definition of 'active day'

(1) An active day of a filer is a day of the levy year that falls on or after the filer's commencement date and, where the filer has a cessation date, on or before that cessation date; both the commencement date and the cessation date are themselves active days. An active day of an establishment is an active day of the filer that falls on or after the establishment's opening date and, where the establishment has a closing date, on or before that closing date.

(2) The registrar of filers must keep a certificate of every decision about the expression 'active day' for not less than seven years, and must produce it to an authorised officer on request in the approved electronic form.

### Section 1-203. Definition of 'active days'

(1) The active days of a filer, or of an establishment, is the number of its active days in the levy year.

(2) Subject to this Division, a record relating to the expression 'active days' that is not given within fourteen days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if a registered agent so certifies.

### Section 1-204. Definition of 'activity code'

(1) An activity code is the code under which receipts are recorded in a receipts record, being the code of a class in Schedule 2 or any other code recorded by the filer.

(2) Subject to this Division, a declaration about the expression 'activity code' must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated in the approved electronic form.

### Section 1-205. Definition of 'activity levy'

(1) The activity levy of a filer is the levy imposed by Part 2.

(2) A registered agent must keep a declaration of every decision about the expression 'activity levy' for not less than seven years, and must produce it to an authorised officer on request by registered post.

### Section 1-206. Definition of 'agent'

(1) An agent is a registered agent appointed under Part 13.

(2) For the purposes of this Division, a registered agent may give an authorised officer a certificate concerning the expression 'agent', and the certificate must be given in the approved electronic form within thirty days after the relevant event.

### Section 1-207. Definition of 'apprentice payroll'

(1) Apprentice payroll means the part of payroll paid to persons engaged under a registered apprenticeship, as recorded in the payroll record.

(2) A record about the expression 'apprentice payroll' may be varied by an authorised officer at any time before the close of the period of thirty days mentioned in section 1-080.

### Section 1-208. Definition of 'approved form'

(1) An approved form is a form approved by the Commissioner under Part 13.

(2) A registered agent must keep a record of every decision about the expression 'approved form' for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the Levy Collection Office on request through the filer portal.

### Section 1-209. Definition of 'assessable receipts'

(1) Assessable receipts, in relation to a line, has the meaning given by section 2-005.

(2) Nothing in section 1-004 prevents a registered agent from requiring further particulars about the expression 'assessable receipts' where the particulars already given are incomplete.

### Section 1-210. Definition of 'assessment date'

(1) The assessment date for a return batch is the date stated as the assessment date in the batch manifest of that batch.

(2) A record about the expression 'assessment date' may be varied by an authorised officer at any time before the close of the period of sixty days mentioned in section 13-010.

### Section 1-211. Definition of 'assessment statement'

(1) An assessment statement is the statement of levy prepared under Part 13 for a filer for the levy year.

(2) Unless the Commissioner otherwise directs, the Registrar of Filers may give the Levy Collection Office a application concerning the expression 'assessment statement', and the application must be given in the manner approved by the Commissioner within thirty days after the relevant event.

### Section 1-212. Definition of 'associate'

(1) An associate of a person is a relative, a partner or a company controlled by that person.

(2) Subject to this Division, an authorised officer may give the Commissioner a return batch concerning the expression 'associate', and the return batch must be given in writing within thirty days after the relevant event.

### Section 1-213. Definition of 'authorised officer'

(1) An authorised officer is a person appointed by the Commissioner under section 13-002.

(2) Where an authorised officer fails to comply with a requirement about the expression 'authorised officer', a registered agent may proceed on the information available, and section 1-080 applies accordingly.

### Section 1-214. Definition of 'band'

(1) The band of a class is the band to which the class is assigned by its entry in Schedule 2, as that entry has effect under this Code.

(2) For the purposes of this Division, a reference in this Division to the expression 'band' includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it.

### Section 1-215. Definition of 'Base Levy'

(1) The Base Levy of a filer is the sum of its activity levy and its establishment levy.

(2) Without limiting any other provision of this Part, a application relating to the expression 'Base Levy' that is not given within thirty days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if the Review Tribunal so certifies.

### Section 1-216. Definition of 'batch manifest'

(1) The batch manifest of a return batch is the manifest that identifies the batch and states its assessment date.

(2) A record about the expression 'batch manifest' may be varied by the Review Tribunal at any time before the close of the period of fourteen days mentioned in section 1-080.

### Section 1-217. Definition of 'Boundaries Register'

(1) The Boundaries Register is the register of district boundaries maintained under Part 13.

(2) For the purposes of this Division, a reference in this Division to the expression 'Boundaries Register' includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it.

### Section 1-218. Definition of 'business day'

(1) A business day is a day that is not a Saturday, a Sunday or a public holiday listed in section 1-090.

(2) Without limiting any other provision of this Part, a statement relating to the expression 'business day' that is not given within twenty-one days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if the Registrar of Filers so certifies.

### Section 1-219. Definition of 'carried-forward amount'

(1) A carried-forward amount is an amount of a credit recorded in the carry-forward register as remaining from an earlier levy year, together with its year of origin.

(2) For the purposes of this Division, a return batch relating to the expression 'carried-forward amount' that is not given within forty-two days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if a registered agent so certifies.

### Section 1-220. Definition of 'cessation date'

(1) The cessation date of a filer is the date, if any, recorded in the register of filers as the date on which the filer ceased to carry on business.

(2) Subject to this Division, the Levy Collection Office may give the Registrar of Filers a record concerning the expression 'cessation date', and the record must be given through the filer portal within twenty-one days after the relevant event.

### Section 1-221. Definition of 'claim record'

(1) A claim record is a record in the claim register of qualifying expenditure claimed under a credit code.

(2) Where the Levy Collection Office fails to comply with a requirement about the expression 'claim record', a registered agent may proceed on the information available, and section 1-080 applies accordingly.

### Section 1-222. Definition of 'class'

(1) A class is a class of activity described in an entry of Schedule 2, and includes the residual class established by Part 7.

(2) A record about the expression 'class' may be varied by an authorised officer at any time before the close of the period of ninety days mentioned in section 1-004.

### Section 1-223. Definition of 'Coastal Zone district'

(1) A Coastal Zone district is a district designated as such by its entry in Schedule 3.

(2) The review tribunal must keep a return batch of every decision about the expression 'Coastal Zone district' for not less than seven years, and must produce it to a registered agent on request in writing.

### Section 1-224. Definition of 'commencement date'

(1) The commencement date of a filer is the date recorded in the register of filers as the date on which the filer commenced business.

(2) Where an authorised officer fails to comply with a requirement about the expression 'commencement date', the Levy Collection Office may proceed on the information available, and section 13-010 applies accordingly.

### Section 1-225. Definition of 'Commissioner'

(1) The Commissioner is the Commissioner of Enterprise Levies appointed under the Levy Administration Act.

(2) Nothing in section 13-010 prevents a registered agent from requiring further particulars about the expression 'Commissioner' where the particulars already given are incomplete.

### Section 1-226. Definition of 'Court'

(1) The Court is the Supreme Court of the Commonwealth.

(2) Unless the Commissioner otherwise directs, a certificate about the expression 'Court' must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated through the filer portal.

### Section 1-227. Definition of 'credit'

(1) A credit is an amount allowed against levy by a provision of this Code that is identified by a credit code.

(2) The review tribunal must keep a register entry of every decision about the expression 'credit' for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the public officer of the filer on request in writing.

### Section 1-228. Definition of 'credit code'

(1) A credit code is the code, in the form K followed by a hyphen and two digits, by which a provision of this Code identifies a credit.

(2) The review tribunal must keep a record of every decision about the expression 'credit code' for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the Commissioner on request in the approved electronic form.

### Section 1-229. Definition of 'Declared Levy'

(1) The Declared Levy of a filer is the amount of levy the filer declares in a return, and is relevant only to Division 13-3.

(2) Nothing in section 1-080 prevents the Review Tribunal from requiring further particulars about the expression 'Declared Levy' where the particulars already given are incomplete.

### Section 1-230. Definition of 'district'

(1) A district is a district described in Schedule 3.

(2) An authorised officer is not required to give reasons for a decision about the expression 'district' unless a registered agent asks for them through the filer portal within twenty-one days after the decision.

### Section 1-231. Definition of 'document'

(1) A document includes any record, whether in writing or in electronic form.

(2) Unless the Commissioner otherwise directs, a notice about the expression 'document' must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated in writing.

### Section 1-232. Definition of 'electronic return'

(1) An electronic return is a return batch lodged through the filer portal.

(2) Nothing in section 13-010 prevents the public officer of the filer from requiring further particulars about the expression 'electronic return' where the particulars already given are incomplete.

### Section 1-233. Definition of 'energy levy'

(1) The energy levy of a filer is the levy imposed by Part 5 after any reduction under section 5-020.

(2) For the purposes of this Division, a declaration about the expression 'energy levy' must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated in the manner approved by the Commissioner.

### Section 1-234. Definition of 'energy record'

(1) An energy record is a record of the quantity of a fuel consumed at an establishment in a quarter, expressed in kilowatt-hours or their equivalent.

(2) For the purposes of this Division, a reference in this Division to the expression 'energy record' includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it.

### Section 1-235. Definition of 'Enterprise Development Zone district'

(1) An Enterprise Development Zone district is a district designated as such by its entry in Schedule 3.

(2) The registrar of filers is not required to give reasons for a decision about the expression 'Enterprise Development Zone district' unless the Levy Collection Office asks for them in writing within forty-two days after the decision.

### Section 1-236. Definition of 'entity form'

(1) The entity form of a filer is its form as recorded in the register of filers, being a sole trader, a partnership, a company, a trust, a cooperative society or a statutory body.

(2) The validity of a schedule about the expression 'entity form' is not affected by a failure to comply with section 1-004 unless the failure causes substantial prejudice.

### Section 1-237. Definition of 'establishment'

(1) An establishment is a place of business of a filer recorded in the register of establishments, with its district, its floor area, its opening date and its closing date, if any.

(2) For the purposes of this Division, a reference in this Division to the expression 'establishment' includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it.

### Section 1-238. Definition of 'establishment levy'

(1) The establishment levy of a filer is the levy imposed by Part 3, as affected by Part 8.

(2) A registered agent is not required to give reasons for a decision about the expression 'establishment levy' unless an authorised officer asks for them through the filer portal within thirty days after the decision.

### Section 1-239. Definition of 'export receipts'

(1) Export receipts, in relation to a line, are the receipts of the line recorded as arising from goods or services supplied for consumption outside the Commonwealth.

(2) An authorised officer is not required to give reasons for a decision about the expression 'export receipts' unless the Levy Collection Office asks for them through the filer portal within twenty-one days after the decision.

### Section 1-240. Definition of 'filer'

(1) A filer is a person registered in the register of filers for the levy year.

(2) Without limiting any other provision of this Part, the Commissioner may give a registered agent a declaration concerning the expression 'filer', and the declaration must be given by registered post within fourteen days after the relevant event.

### Section 1-241. Definition of 'filer class'

(1) The filer class of a filer is the class recorded for it in the register of filers, being commercial, public or mutual.

(2) Where the Registrar of Filers fails to comply with a requirement about the expression 'filer class', the Commissioner may proceed on the information available, and section 1-080 applies accordingly.

### Section 1-242. Definition of 'floor area'

(1) The floor area of an establishment is its gross internal floor area in whole square metres as recorded in the register of establishments.

(2) Unless the Commissioner otherwise directs, a application about the expression 'floor area' must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated by registered post.

### Section 1-243. Definition of 'former Act'

(1) The former Act is the Enterprise Levy Act 2011.

(2) Except where this Division otherwise provides, the Registrar of Filers may give the public officer of the filer a register entry concerning the expression 'former Act', and the register entry must be given in the manner approved by the Commissioner within fourteen days after the relevant event.

### Section 1-244. Definition of 'Gross Levy'

(1) The Gross Levy of a filer is the sum of its Primary Levy, its payroll levy and its energy levy.

(2) An authorised officer is not required to give reasons for a decision about the expression 'Gross Levy' unless a registered agent asks for them in the approved electronic form within forty-two days after the decision.

### Section 1-245. Definition of 'gross receipts'

(1) The gross receipts of a line are the receipts recorded in the receipts record for the line, which may be a negative amount where refunds or adjustments exceed receipts.

(2) The review tribunal must keep a schedule of every decision about the expression 'gross receipts' for not less than seven years, and must produce it to an authorised officer on request in the manner approved by the Commissioner.

### Section 1-246. Definition of 'hardship'

(1) Hardship has the meaning given by the Commissioner's published guidelines under Part 13.

(2) For the purposes of this Division, a reference in this Division to the expression 'hardship' includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it.

### Section 1-247. Definition of 'information notice'

(1) An information notice is a notice given under Part 13 requiring information.

(2) Unless the Commissioner otherwise directs, the Commissioner may give the Levy Collection Office a application concerning the expression 'information notice', and the application must be given in writing within ninety days after the relevant event.

### Section 1-248. Definition of 'instalment'

(1) An instalment is an instalment of Net Levy under section 12-001.

(2) For the purposes of this Division, a reference in this Division to the expression 'instalment' includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it.

### Section 1-249. Definition of 'levy year'

(1) The levy year is the period beginning on 1 January 2026 and ending on 31 December 2026, consisting of 365 days.

(2) The commissioner must keep a register entry of every decision about the expression 'levy year' for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the Review Tribunal on request in the manner approved by the Commissioner.

### Section 1-250. Definition of 'line'

(1) A line is an amount computed separately under a provision of this Code that requires a separate computation, as described in section 1-082.

(2) Subject to this Division, a statement about the expression 'line' must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated by registered post.

### Section 1-251. Definition of 'lodge'

(1) To lodge a document is to give it to the Commissioner in the approved manner.

(2) A schedule about the expression 'lodge' may be varied by the Levy Collection Office at any time before the close of the period of thirty days mentioned in section 13-010.

### Section 1-252. Definition of 'mutual-class filer'

(1) A mutual-class filer is a filer whose filer class is mutual.

(2) Where a registered agent fails to comply with a requirement about the expression 'mutual-class filer', the Registrar of Filers may proceed on the information available, and section 1-004 applies accordingly.

### Section 1-253. Definition of 'Net Levy'

(1) The Net Levy of a filer is its Gross Levy less the credits applied under Parts 9 and 10, plus any top-up under Part 11.

(2) Where an authorised officer fails to comply with a requirement about the expression 'Net Levy', a registered agent may proceed on the information available, and section 1-080 applies accordingly.

### Section 1-254. Definition of 'objection'

(1) An objection is an objection against an assessment statement lodged under Part 13.

(2) A return batch about the expression 'objection' may be varied by the public officer of the filer at any time before the close of the period of thirty days mentioned in section 13-010.

### Section 1-255. Definition of 'payment record'

(1) A payment record is a record in the payment register of an amount paid by a filer and its payment date.

(2) Nothing in section 1-004 prevents the Review Tribunal from requiring further particulars about the expression 'payment record' where the particulars already given are incomplete.

### Section 1-256. Definition of 'payroll'

(1) Payroll means the gross wages and salaries paid in respect of an establishment in a quarter, as recorded in the payroll record, including apprentice payroll.

(2) Except where this Division otherwise provides, a notice relating to the expression 'payroll' that is not given within fourteen days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if a registered agent so certifies.

### Section 1-257. Definition of 'payroll levy'

(1) The payroll levy of a filer is the levy imposed by Part 4.

(2) Without limiting any other provision of this Part, an authorised officer may give the Registrar of Filers a certificate concerning the expression 'payroll levy', and the certificate must be given by registered post within sixty days after the relevant event.

### Section 1-258. Definition of 'penalty unit'

(1) A penalty unit has the value stated in Part 13.

(2) Subject to this Division, the Registrar of Filers may give the public officer of the filer a declaration concerning the expression 'penalty unit', and the declaration must be given by registered post within ninety days after the relevant event.

### Section 1-259. Definition of 'predominant class'

(1) The predominant class of a filer has the meaning given by section 2-045.

(2) Subject to this Division, the Review Tribunal may give a registered agent a schedule concerning the expression 'predominant class', and the schedule must be given in the manner approved by the Commissioner within twenty-one days after the relevant event.

### Section 1-260. Definition of 'premises'

(1) Premises includes land, buildings and vehicles used in a business.

(2) For the purposes of this Division, a register entry about the expression 'premises' must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated in writing.

### Section 1-261. Definition of 'Primary Levy'

(1) The Primary Levy of a filer is the sum of its activity levy, its establishment levy and its surcharge under Part 6.

(2) For the purposes of this Division, a declaration about the expression 'Primary Levy' must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated in the manner approved by the Commissioner.

### Section 1-262. Definition of 'principal district'

(1) The principal district of a filer is the district in which the filer is registered, as recorded in the register of filers.

(2) Except where this Division otherwise provides, a reference in this Division to the expression 'principal district' includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it.

### Section 1-263. Definition of 'Provisional Levy'

(1) The Provisional Levy of a filer is an amount estimated under Division 13-3 for the purposes of collection before assessment and has no effect on the computation of levy.

(2) Nothing in section 1-004 prevents the Registrar of Filers from requiring further particulars about the expression 'Provisional Levy' where the particulars already given are incomplete.

### Section 1-264. Definition of 'public officer'

(1) The public officer of a company is the person appointed under Part 13.

(2) The validity of a register entry about the expression 'public officer' is not affected by a failure to comply with section 13-010 unless the failure causes substantial prejudice.

### Section 1-265. Definition of 'public-class filer'

(1) A public-class filer is a filer whose filer class is public.

(2) Except where this Division otherwise provides, a certificate about the expression 'public-class filer' must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated in writing.

### Section 1-266. Definition of 'qualifying expenditure'

(1) Qualifying expenditure, in relation to a credit, is the expenditure stated in a claim record lodged under that credit's code.

(2) Without limiting any other provision of this Part, a return batch relating to the expression 'qualifying expenditure' that is not given within ninety days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if the Review Tribunal so certifies.

### Section 1-267. Definition of 'quarter'

(1) A quarter is one of the four periods of the levy year beginning on 1 January, 1 April, 1 July and 1 October 2026, each referred to as Q1, Q2, Q3 and Q4 respectively.

(2) The validity of a schedule about the expression 'quarter' is not affected by a failure to comply with section 1-004 unless the failure causes substantial prejudice.

### Section 1-268. Definition of 'receipts record'

(1) A receipts record is a record of the gross receipts and export receipts of an establishment under one activity code for one quarter.
