(2) Unless the Commissioner otherwise directs, an authorised officer may give the Registrar of Filers a schedule concerning the expression 'receipts record', and the schedule must be given through the filer portal within forty-two days after the relevant event.

### Section 1-269. Definition of 'record'

(1) A record includes a register entry.

(2) The validity of a certificate about the expression 'record' is not affected by a failure to comply with section 1-004 unless the failure causes substantial prejudice.

### Section 1-270. Definition of 'region'

(1) A region is one of the North, South, West and East regions, and the region of a district is the region stated in its entry in Schedule 3.

(2) The commissioner is not required to give reasons for a decision about the expression 'region' unless a registered agent asks for them in the approved electronic form within sixty days after the decision.

### Section 1-271. Definition of 'register of establishments'

(1) The register of establishments is the register of the establishments of filers.

(2) Unless the Commissioner otherwise directs, a register entry about the expression 'register of establishments' must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated through the filer portal.

### Section 1-272. Definition of 'register of filers'

(1) The register of filers is the register of filers maintained by the Registrar of Filers.

(2) Unless the Commissioner otherwise directs, a reference in this Division to the expression 'register of filers' includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it.

### Section 1-273. Definition of 'registered district'

(1) The registered district of a filer is the district recorded for it in the register of filers.

(2) Where the Commissioner fails to comply with a requirement about the expression 'registered district', a registered agent may proceed on the information available, and section 1-004 applies accordingly.

### Section 1-274. Definition of 'Registrar of Filers'

(1) The Registrar of Filers is the officer appointed under Part 13.

(2) Except where this Division otherwise provides, a reference in this Division to the expression 'Registrar of Filers' includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it.

### Section 1-275. Definition of 'return batch'

(1) A return batch is the set of registers and records lodged together for assessment, identified by its batch manifest.

(2) A declaration about the expression 'return batch' may be varied by the Levy Collection Office at any time before the close of the period of ninety days mentioned in section 13-010.

### Section 1-276. Definition of 'Review Tribunal'

(1) The Review Tribunal is the Levy Review Tribunal.

(2) The public officer of the filer is not required to give reasons for a decision about the expression 'Review Tribunal' unless the Levy Collection Office asks for them in the manner approved by the Commissioner within ninety days after the decision.

### Section 1-277. Definition of 'scheduled rate'

(1) The scheduled rate of a class for a quarter is the rate stated in the entry for the class, as that rate has effect under this Code for that quarter.

(2) Where the Commissioner so requires, a reference in this Division to the expression 'scheduled rate' includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it.

### Section 1-278. Definition of 'small enterprise election'

(1) A small enterprise election is an election recorded in the register of filers to be assessed under section 2-040.

(2) Nothing in section 13-010 prevents the Review Tribunal from requiring further particulars about the expression 'small enterprise election' where the particulars already given are incomplete.

### Section 1-279. Definition of 'statutory body'

(1) A statutory body is a body corporate established by an Act of the Commonwealth.

(2) A registered agent is not required to give reasons for a decision about the expression 'statutory body' unless the public officer of the filer asks for them in the manner approved by the Commissioner within ninety days after the decision.

### Section 1-280. Definition of 'surcharge'

(1) The surcharge of a filer is the amount imposed by Part 6.

(2) Nothing in section 1-080 prevents the Review Tribunal from requiring further particulars about the expression 'surcharge' where the particulars already given are incomplete.

### Section 1-281. Definition of 'tax agent'

(1) A tax agent is a person registered as a tax agent under the Tax Agent Services Act.

(2) The public officer of the filer is not required to give reasons for a decision about the expression 'tax agent' unless the Commissioner asks for them in the approved electronic form within twenty-eight days after the decision.

### Section 1-282. Definition of 'top-up'

(1) A top-up is the amount added to levy under Part 11.

(2) Except where this Division otherwise provides, a return batch about the expression 'top-up' must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated through the filer portal.

## Part 2. Activity Levy

### Section 2-001. Imposition of activity levy

(1) Activity levy is imposed on the assessable receipts of every line of a filer.

(2) The activity levy of a filer is the sum of the activity levy of its lines.

### Section 2-003. Classes

(1) The classes of activity are described in the entries of Schedule 2, being sections 2-101 to 2-160, and in the provision establishing the residual class.

(2) A line is assessed under the class whose code is the activity code of the receipts record, except where Part 7 otherwise provides.

### Section 2-005. Assessable receipts

(1) The assessable receipts of a line are its gross receipts.

(2) Where the entry of the class under which the line is assessed provides that export receipts are excluded, the export receipts of the line are deducted from its gross receipts.

### Section 2-007. Lines of activity levy

(1) Each receipts record is a separate line.

(2) The quarter of a line is the quarter stated in its receipts record.

### Section 2-010. Computation

(1) The activity levy of a line is its assessable receipts multiplied by the scheduled rate of its class in force for the quarter of the line, rounded under section 1-080.

(2) Section 1-088 applies to a line whose assessable receipts are nil or negative.

### Section 2-040. Small enterprise rate

(1) Where (a) a filer has made a small enterprise election, and (b) the total assessable receipts of the filer for the levy year do not exceed $250,000, the activity levy of every line of the filer is computed at 60 per cent of the scheduled rate.

(2) For subsection (1)(b), the total assessable receipts of a filer is the sum of the assessable receipts of its lines, disregarding any line whose assessable receipts are nil or negative.

(3) The rate under subsection (1) is the scheduled rate multiplied by 60 and divided by 100, and is applied to each line before rounding.

### Section 2-041. Public-class filers

(1) A public-class filer may not make a small enterprise election.

(2) A small enterprise election recorded for a public-class filer has no effect, and the Commissioner must record that the election was disregarded.

### Section 2-045. Predominant class

(1) The predominant class of a filer is the class under which the greatest total of assessable receipts of its lines is assessed, disregarding lines whose assessable receipts are nil or negative.

(2) Where two or more classes have the same greatest total, the predominant class is the one with the lowest class code.

(3) A filer none of whose lines has positive assessable receipts has no predominant class and no band.

### Section 2-050. Classification disputes

(1) Unless the Commissioner otherwise directs, a reference in this Division to classification disputes includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it. The validity of a record about classification disputes is not affected by a failure to comply with section 13-014 unless the failure causes substantial prejudice. A declaration about classification disputes may be varied by the Review Tribunal at any time before the close of the period of forty-two days mentioned in section 2-003. Where the Commissioner so requires, a certificate about classification disputes must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated in the manner approved by the Commissioner.

(2) Where the Commissioner so requires, a reference in this Division to classification disputes includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it. Where the Commissioner so requires, a reference in this Division to classification disputes includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it.

### Section 2-052. Receipts recorded under more than one class

(1) Without limiting any other provision of this Part, a application about receipts recorded under more than one class must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated by registered post. A declaration about receipts recorded under more than one class may be varied by an authorised officer at any time before the close of the period of ninety days mentioned in section 13-014. A certificate about receipts recorded under more than one class may be varied by the Registrar of Filers at any time before the close of the period of ninety days mentioned in section 13-014.

(2) Unless the Commissioner otherwise directs, a reference in this Division to receipts recorded under more than one class includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it. Where the Commissioner so requires, the Review Tribunal may give the Levy Collection Office a application concerning receipts recorded under more than one class, and the application must be given through the filer portal within sixty days after the relevant event.

(3) Where the public officer of the filer fails to comply with a requirement about receipts recorded under more than one class, the Review Tribunal may proceed on the information available, and section 2-003 applies accordingly. Except where this Division otherwise provides, a statement about receipts recorded under more than one class must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated through the filer portal. The levy collection office must keep a statement of every decision about receipts recorded under more than one class for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the Commissioner on request by registered post.

### Section 2-054. The records of receipts

(1) Nothing in section 2-003 prevents a registered agent from requiring further particulars about the records of receipts where the particulars already given are incomplete. Subject to this Division, a register entry about the records of receipts must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated in the approved electronic form.

(2) Where the Commissioner so requires, a statement about the records of receipts must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated in the manner approved by the Commissioner. The validity of a statement about the records of receipts is not affected by a failure to comply with section 13-014 unless the failure causes substantial prejudice. Unless the Commissioner otherwise directs, a reference in this Division to the records of receipts includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it. Unless the Commissioner otherwise directs, a notice about the records of receipts must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated in the manner approved by the Commissioner.

(3) Where a registered agent fails to comply with a requirement about the records of receipts, the Commissioner may proceed on the information available, and section 13-014 applies accordingly. Where a registered agent fails to comply with a requirement about the records of receipts, the Commissioner may proceed on the information available, and section 13-014 applies accordingly.

(4) Unless the Commissioner otherwise directs, a declaration relating to the records of receipts that is not given within ninety days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if the Levy Collection Office so certifies. A return batch about the records of receipts may be varied by the Review Tribunal at any time before the close of the period of fourteen days mentioned in section 13-014. The levy collection office must keep a register entry of every decision about the records of receipts for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the Registrar of Filers on request in the manner approved by the Commissioner. Unless the Commissioner otherwise directs, the Review Tribunal may give the Registrar of Filers a return batch concerning the records of receipts, and the return batch must be given in writing within sixty days after the relevant event.

### Section 2-056. Estimates of receipts

(1) Nothing in section 13-014 prevents the Registrar of Filers from requiring further particulars about estimates of receipts where the particulars already given are incomplete. Except where this Division otherwise provides, a registered agent may give an authorised officer a certificate concerning estimates of receipts, and the certificate must be given in writing within forty-two days after the relevant event.

(2) For the purposes of this Division, a application about estimates of receipts must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated in the approved electronic form. Where the Commissioner so requires, a application relating to estimates of receipts that is not given within forty-two days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if an authorised officer so certifies. For the purposes of this Division, a statement relating to estimates of receipts that is not given within ninety days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if the public officer of the filer so certifies. The review tribunal is not required to give reasons for a decision about estimates of receipts unless the Levy Collection Office asks for them by registered post within thirty days after the decision.

(3) For the purposes of this Division, the Review Tribunal may give the public officer of the filer a schedule concerning estimates of receipts, and the schedule must be given by registered post within forty-two days after the relevant event. The validity of a declaration about estimates of receipts is not affected by a failure to comply with section 13-014 unless the failure causes substantial prejudice. The levy collection office is not required to give reasons for a decision about estimates of receipts unless the Review Tribunal asks for them by registered post within twenty-one days after the decision. Subject to this Division, the Registrar of Filers may give the Review Tribunal a return batch concerning estimates of receipts, and the return batch must be given through the filer portal within sixty days after the relevant event.

(4) The public officer of the filer is not required to give reasons for a decision about estimates of receipts unless a registered agent asks for them by registered post within forty-two days after the decision. Except where this Division otherwise provides, a reference in this Division to estimates of receipts includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it. Subject to this Division, a record about estimates of receipts must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated through the filer portal.

#### Schedule 2. Classes of Activity

### Section 2-101. Class AC-101: Cold storage and refrigerated warehousing

(a) Scope. This class comprises the operating of cold storage and refrigerated warehousing as a business, whether or not for profit, and whether carried on from fixed premises or otherwise, where that activity is the activity to which the receipts of the line relate. An activity is within this class only where the receipts concerned arise from the activity itself and not from an investment, a sale of a capital asset or a grant.

(b) Inclusions. Without limiting paragraph (a), this class includes the operation of premises used mainly for the activity, the provision of advice, design or supervision connected with the activity, the licensing of rights arising from the activity and the transport of goods to or from the activity, in each case only where the person carrying on cold storage and refrigerated warehousing also carries on that ancillary activity at the same establishment and records its receipts with the receipts of the class.

(c) Exclusions. This class does not include an activity described in Class AC-154 (footwear and leather goods), Class AC-151 (agricultural contracting and harvesting) or Class AC-145 (real estate agency and property management), and receipts of such an activity are to be recorded under the code of the class in which that activity is described.

(d) Scheduled rate. For the purposes of section 2-010 the scheduled rate of this class is 0.1800 per cent.

(e) Export receipts. The export receipts of a line assessed under this class are deducted in computing its assessable receipts under section 2-005.

(f) Band. This class is assigned to Band I.

(g) Records. The validity of a declaration about receipts of cold storage and refrigerated warehousing is not affected by a failure to comply with section 2-003 unless the failure causes substantial prejudice. Where the Commissioner so requires, the Commissioner may give the Review Tribunal a declaration concerning receipts of cold storage and refrigerated warehousing, and the declaration must be given by registered post within twenty-eight days after the relevant event.

(h) Classification notes. Where a registered agent fails to comply with a requirement about the classification of cold storage and refrigerated warehousing, the Review Tribunal may proceed on the information available, and section 13-057 applies accordingly. The validity of a register entry about the classification of cold storage and refrigerated warehousing is not affected by a failure to comply with section 13-057 unless the failure causes substantial prejudice.

### Section 2-102. Class AC-102: Commercial offset and digital printing

(a) Scope. This class comprises the conducting of commercial offset and digital printing as a business, whether or not for profit, and whether carried on from fixed premises or otherwise, where that activity is the activity to which the receipts of the line relate. An activity is within this class only where the receipts concerned arise from the activity itself and not from an investment, a sale of a capital asset or a grant.

(b) Inclusions. Without limiting paragraph (a), this class includes the collection of fees for access to the activity, the sale of goods incidental to the activity, the transport of goods to or from the activity and the provision of training in the activity, in each case only where the person carrying on commercial offset and digital printing also carries on that ancillary activity at the same establishment and records its receipts with the receipts of the class.

(c) Exclusions. This class does not include an activity described in Class AC-146 (self-storage facilities), Class AC-158 (fitness centres and sports facilities) or Class AC-144 (commercial fit-out and interior works), and receipts of such an activity are to be recorded under the code of the class in which that activity is described.

(d) Scheduled rate. Assessable receipts of a line assessed under this class bear activity levy at the scheduled rate of 0.4600 per cent.

(e) Export receipts. Export receipts of a line assessed under this class are not excluded from assessable receipts.

(f) Band. This class is assigned to Band I.

(g) Records. Without limiting any other provision of this Part, the Registrar of Filers may give an authorised officer a return batch concerning receipts of commercial offset and digital printing, and the return batch must be given through the filer portal within twenty-eight days after the relevant event. Where the Levy Collection Office fails to comply with a requirement about receipts of commercial offset and digital printing, the Review Tribunal may proceed on the information available, and section 2-003 applies accordingly.

(h) Classification notes. The commissioner is not required to give reasons for a decision about the classification of commercial offset and digital printing unless the Levy Collection Office asks for them in the approved electronic form within twenty-one days after the decision. Where the Commissioner so requires, a return batch relating to the classification of commercial offset and digital printing that is not given within sixty days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if the Levy Collection Office so certifies.

### Section 2-103. Class AC-103: Freight forwarding and customs brokerage

(a) Scope. This class comprises the conducting of freight forwarding and customs brokerage as a business, whether or not for profit, and whether carried on from fixed premises or otherwise, where that activity is the activity to which the receipts of the line relate. An activity is within this class only where the receipts concerned arise from the activity itself and not from an investment, a sale of a capital asset or a grant.

(b) Inclusions. Without limiting paragraph (a), this class includes the provision of training in the activity, the collection of fees for access to the activity, the transport of goods to or from the activity and the provision of advice, design or supervision connected with the activity, in each case only where the person carrying on freight forwarding and customs brokerage also carries on that ancillary activity at the same establishment and records its receipts with the receipts of the class.

(c) Exclusions. This class does not include an activity described in Class AC-150 (rail freight handling), Class AC-152 (horticultural nurseries) or Class AC-110 (food service and contract catering), and receipts of such an activity are to be recorded under the code of the class in which that activity is described.

(d) Scheduled rate. The scheduled rate for this class is 0.6000 per cent.

(e) Export receipts. Export receipts of a line assessed under this class are not excluded from assessable receipts.

(f) Band. This class is assigned to Band II.

(g) Records. Except where this Division otherwise provides, the Review Tribunal may give the Commissioner a schedule concerning receipts of freight forwarding and customs brokerage, and the schedule must be given through the filer portal within sixty days after the relevant event. A certificate about receipts of freight forwarding and customs brokerage may be varied by the Commissioner at any time before the close of the period of twenty-one days mentioned in section 2-003.

(h) Classification notes. A registered agent must keep a statement of every decision about the classification of freight forwarding and customs brokerage for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the Levy Collection Office on request in writing. A registered agent is not required to give reasons for a decision about the classification of freight forwarding and customs brokerage unless the public officer of the filer asks for them through the filer portal within ninety days after the decision.

### Section 2-104. Class AC-104: Retail sale of pharmaceutical goods

(a) Scope. This class comprises the carrying on of retail sale of pharmaceutical goods as a business, whether or not for profit, and whether carried on from fixed premises or otherwise, where that activity is the activity to which the receipts of the line relate. An activity is within this class only where the receipts concerned arise from the activity itself and not from an investment, a sale of a capital asset or a grant.

(b) Inclusions. Without limiting paragraph (a), this class includes the provision of training in the activity, the provision of advice, design or supervision connected with the activity, the sale of goods incidental to the activity and the maintenance of plant used in the activity, in each case only where the person carrying on retail sale of pharmaceutical goods also carries on that ancillary activity at the same establishment and records its receipts with the receipts of the class.

(c) Exclusions. This class does not include an activity described in Class AC-126 (cinema and live entertainment venues), Class AC-106 (architectural and engineering consultancy) or Class AC-135 (market research and polling), and receipts of such an activity are to be recorded under the code of the class in which that activity is described.

(d) Scheduled rate. The scheduled rate for this class is 0.9950 per cent.

(e) Export receipts. Export receipts of a line assessed under this class are not excluded from assessable receipts.

(f) Band. This class is assigned to Band II.

(g) Records. For the purposes of this Division, a declaration about receipts of retail sale of pharmaceutical goods must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated through the filer portal. A registered agent must keep a schedule of every decision about receipts of retail sale of pharmaceutical goods for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the public officer of the filer on request by registered post.

(h) Classification notes. Without limiting any other provision of this Part, a register entry about the classification of retail sale of pharmaceutical goods must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated in writing. Unless the Commissioner otherwise directs, a certificate relating to the classification of retail sale of pharmaceutical goods that is not given within fourteen days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if the Levy Collection Office so certifies.

### Section 2-105. Class AC-105: Wholesale distribution of building materials

(a) Scope. This class comprises the operating of wholesale distribution of building materials as a business, whether or not for profit, and whether carried on from fixed premises or otherwise, where that activity is the activity to which the receipts of the line relate. An activity is within this class only where the receipts concerned arise from the activity itself and not from an investment, a sale of a capital asset or a grant.

(b) Inclusions. Without limiting paragraph (a), this class includes the licensing of rights arising from the activity, the provision of training in the activity, the sale of goods incidental to the activity and the provision of advice, design or supervision connected with the activity, in each case only where the person carrying on wholesale distribution of building materials also carries on that ancillary activity at the same establishment and records its receipts with the receipts of the class.

(c) Exclusions. This class does not include an activity described in Class AC-111 (veterinary and animal health services), Class AC-123 (pulp, paper and paperboard manufacture) or Class AC-127 (private tuition and vocational training), and receipts of such an activity are to be recorded under the code of the class in which that activity is described.

(d) Scheduled rate. The scheduled rate for this class is 0.9200 per cent.

(e) Export receipts. Export receipts of a line assessed under this class are not excluded from assessable receipts.

(f) Band. This class is assigned to Band I.

(g) Records. Where the Commissioner so requires, a schedule relating to receipts of wholesale distribution of building materials that is not given within twenty-one days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if the Commissioner so certifies. The validity of a record about receipts of wholesale distribution of building materials is not affected by a failure to comply with section 2-003 unless the failure causes substantial prejudice.

(h) Classification notes. The validity of a schedule about the classification of wholesale distribution of building materials is not affected by a failure to comply with section 13-057 unless the failure causes substantial prejudice. Except where this Division otherwise provides, the Commissioner may give an authorised officer a declaration concerning the classification of wholesale distribution of building materials, and the declaration must be given by registered post within forty-two days after the relevant event.

### Section 2-106. Class AC-106: Architectural and engineering consultancy

(a) Scope. This class comprises the undertaking of architectural and engineering consultancy as a business, whether or not for profit, and whether carried on from fixed premises or otherwise, where that activity is the activity to which the receipts of the line relate. An activity is within this class only where the receipts concerned arise from the activity itself and not from an investment, a sale of a capital asset or a grant.

(b) Inclusions. Without limiting paragraph (a), this class includes the licensing of rights arising from the activity, the collection of fees for access to the activity, the transport of goods to or from the activity and the provision of advice, design or supervision connected with the activity, in each case only where the person carrying on architectural and engineering consultancy also carries on that ancillary activity at the same establishment and records its receipts with the receipts of the class.

(c) Exclusions. This class does not include an activity described in Class AC-154 (footwear and leather goods), Class AC-149 (marine charter and harbour services) or Class AC-131 (dental and orthodontic practice), and receipts of such an activity are to be recorded under the code of the class in which that activity is described.

(d) Scheduled rate. Assessable receipts of a line assessed under this class bear activity levy at the scheduled rate of 1.4000 per cent.

(e) Export receipts. No deduction is made under section 2-005 for the export receipts of a line assessed under this class.

(f) Band. This class is assigned to Band II.

(g) Records. For the purposes of this Division, the Registrar of Filers may give the Review Tribunal a schedule concerning receipts of architectural and engineering consultancy, and the schedule must be given in the approved electronic form within ninety days after the relevant event. The review tribunal must keep a register entry of every decision about receipts of architectural and engineering consultancy for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the Commissioner on request by registered post.

(h) Classification notes. A registered agent must keep a statement of every decision about the classification of architectural and engineering consultancy for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the public officer of the filer on request in writing. Where the Levy Collection Office fails to comply with a requirement about the classification of architectural and engineering consultancy, the Registrar of Filers may proceed on the information available, and section 2-050 applies accordingly.

### Section 2-107. Class AC-107: Commercial laundering and linen hire

(a) Scope. This class comprises the undertaking of commercial laundering and linen hire as a business, whether or not for profit, and whether carried on from fixed premises or otherwise, where that activity is the activity to which the receipts of the line relate. An activity is within this class only where the receipts concerned arise from the activity itself and not from an investment, a sale of a capital asset or a grant.

(b) Inclusions. Without limiting paragraph (a), this class includes the provision of advice, design or supervision connected with the activity, the collection of fees for access to the activity, the hire of equipment used in the activity and the supply of labour engaged in the activity, in each case only where the person carrying on commercial laundering and linen hire also carries on that ancillary activity at the same establishment and records its receipts with the receipts of the class.

(c) Exclusions. This class does not include an activity described in Class AC-147 (courier and parcel delivery), Class AC-136 (recruitment and labour hire) or Class AC-123 (pulp, paper and paperboard manufacture), and receipts of such an activity are to be recorded under the code of the class in which that activity is described.

(d) Scheduled rate. For the purposes of section 2-010 the scheduled rate of this class is 1.0050 per cent.

(e) Export receipts. Export receipts of a line assessed under this class are not excluded from assessable receipts.

(f) Band. For the purposes of Parts 5 and 9 this class is a Band III class.

(g) Records. Without limiting any other provision of this Part, a reference in this Division to receipts of commercial laundering and linen hire includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it. For the purposes of this Division, a record relating to receipts of commercial laundering and linen hire that is not given within thirty days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if the Commissioner so certifies.

(h) Classification notes. The review tribunal is not required to give reasons for a decision about the classification of commercial laundering and linen hire unless a registered agent asks for them in writing within sixty days after the decision. Except where this Division otherwise provides, a reference in this Division to the classification of commercial laundering and linen hire includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it.

### Section 2-108. Class AC-108: Passenger coach and charter transport

(a) Scope. This class comprises the undertaking of passenger coach and charter transport as a business, whether or not for profit, and whether carried on from fixed premises or otherwise, where that activity is the activity to which the receipts of the line relate. An activity is within this class only where the receipts concerned arise from the activity itself and not from an investment, a sale of a capital asset or a grant.

(b) Inclusions. Without limiting paragraph (a), this class includes the sale of goods incidental to the activity, the transport of goods to or from the activity, the supply of labour engaged in the activity and the hire of equipment used in the activity, in each case only where the person carrying on passenger coach and charter transport also carries on that ancillary activity at the same establishment and records its receipts with the receipts of the class.

(c) Exclusions. This class does not include an activity described in Class AC-110 (food service and contract catering), Class AC-118 (retail sale of motor vehicles) or Class AC-130 (medical imaging and pathology), and receipts of such an activity are to be recorded under the code of the class in which that activity is described.

(d) Scheduled rate. For the purposes of section 2-010 the scheduled rate of this class is 0.8750 per cent.

(e) Export receipts. Export receipts of a line assessed under this class are not excluded from assessable receipts.

(f) Band. For the purposes of Parts 5 and 9 this class is a Band III class.

(g) Records. Unless the Commissioner otherwise directs, the Review Tribunal may give the public officer of the filer a schedule concerning receipts of passenger coach and charter transport, and the schedule must be given by registered post within twenty-eight days after the relevant event. Where the Registrar of Filers fails to comply with a requirement about receipts of passenger coach and charter transport, the Review Tribunal may proceed on the information available, and section 13-014 applies accordingly.

(h) Classification notes. A certificate about the classification of passenger coach and charter transport may be varied by the Review Tribunal at any time before the close of the period of fourteen days mentioned in section 13-057. Subject to this Division, a reference in this Division to the classification of passenger coach and charter transport includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it.

### Section 2-109. Class AC-109: Industrial equipment hire and leasing

(a) Scope. This class comprises the operating of industrial equipment hire and leasing as a business, whether or not for profit, and whether carried on from fixed premises or otherwise, where that activity is the activity to which the receipts of the line relate. An activity is within this class only where the receipts concerned arise from the activity itself and not from an investment, a sale of a capital asset or a grant.

(b) Inclusions. Without limiting paragraph (a), this class includes the provision of advice, design or supervision connected with the activity, the provision of training in the activity, the sale of goods incidental to the activity and the licensing of rights arising from the activity, in each case only where the person carrying on industrial equipment hire and leasing also carries on that ancillary activity at the same establishment and records its receipts with the receipts of the class.

(c) Exclusions. This class does not include an activity described in Class AC-101 (cold storage and refrigerated warehousing), Class AC-124 (brewing and distilling) or Class AC-152 (horticultural nurseries), and receipts of such an activity are to be recorded under the code of the class in which that activity is described.

(d) Scheduled rate. Assessable receipts of a line assessed under this class bear activity levy at the scheduled rate of 0.6850 per cent.

(e) Export receipts. No deduction is made under section 2-005 for the export receipts of a line assessed under this class.

(f) Band. For the purposes of Parts 5 and 9 this class is a Band III class.

(g) Records. Nothing in section 2-003 prevents the Registrar of Filers from requiring further particulars about receipts of industrial equipment hire and leasing where the particulars already given are incomplete. The public officer of the filer must keep a record of every decision about receipts of industrial equipment hire and leasing for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the Commissioner on request in the approved electronic form.

(h) Classification notes. Without limiting any other provision of this Part, a return batch about the classification of industrial equipment hire and leasing must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated in writing. The commissioner is not required to give reasons for a decision about the classification of industrial equipment hire and leasing unless the Review Tribunal asks for them in the approved electronic form within forty-two days after the decision.

### Section 2-110. Class AC-110: Food service and contract catering

(a) Scope. This class comprises the carrying on of food service and contract catering as a business, whether or not for profit, and whether carried on from fixed premises or otherwise, where that activity is the activity to which the receipts of the line relate. An activity is within this class only where the receipts concerned arise from the activity itself and not from an investment, a sale of a capital asset or a grant.

(b) Inclusions. Without limiting paragraph (a), this class includes the supply of labour engaged in the activity, the provision of training in the activity, the licensing of rights arising from the activity and the provision of advice, design or supervision connected with the activity, in each case only where the person carrying on food service and contract catering also carries on that ancillary activity at the same establishment and records its receipts with the receipts of the class.

(c) Exclusions. This class does not include an activity described in Class AC-123 (pulp, paper and paperboard manufacture), Class AC-134 (advertising and media buying) or Class AC-137 (cleaning and facility maintenance), and receipts of such an activity are to be recorded under the code of the class in which that activity is described.

(d) Scheduled rate. Assessable receipts of a line assessed under this class bear activity levy at the scheduled rate of 0.4900 per cent.

(e) Export receipts. No deduction is made under section 2-005 for the export receipts of a line assessed under this class.

(f) Band. This class is assigned to Band I.

(g) Records. The validity of a return batch about receipts of food service and contract catering is not affected by a failure to comply with section 13-014 unless the failure causes substantial prejudice. Where the public officer of the filer fails to comply with a requirement about receipts of food service and contract catering, the Levy Collection Office may proceed on the information available, and section 13-015 applies accordingly.

(h) Classification notes. Without limiting any other provision of this Part, a declaration relating to the classification of food service and contract catering that is not given within thirty days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if the public officer of the filer so certifies. The public officer of the filer must keep a register entry of every decision about the classification of food service and contract catering for not less than seven years, and must produce it to an authorised officer on request in the manner approved by the Commissioner.

### Section 2-111. Class AC-111: Veterinary and animal health services

(a) Scope. This class comprises the conducting of veterinary and animal health services as a business, whether or not for profit, and whether carried on from fixed premises or otherwise, where that activity is the activity to which the receipts of the line relate. An activity is within this class only where the receipts concerned arise from the activity itself and not from an investment, a sale of a capital asset or a grant.

(b) Inclusions. Without limiting paragraph (a), this class includes the provision of advice, design or supervision connected with the activity, the licensing of rights arising from the activity, the supply of labour engaged in the activity and the operation of premises used mainly for the activity, in each case only where the person carrying on veterinary and animal health services also carries on that ancillary activity at the same establishment and records its receipts with the receipts of the class.

(c) Exclusions. This class does not include an activity described in Class AC-103 (freight forwarding and customs brokerage), Class AC-155 (jewellery and precious metal trading) or Class AC-120 (hotel and serviced apartment accommodation), and receipts of such an activity are to be recorded under the code of the class in which that activity is described.

(d) Scheduled rate. For the purposes of section 2-010 the scheduled rate of this class is 0.8900 per cent.

(e) Export receipts. Export receipts of a line assessed under this class are excluded from assessable receipts.

(f) Band. For the purposes of Parts 5 and 9 this class is a Band I class.

(g) Records. Nothing in section 2-003 prevents an authorised officer from requiring further particulars about receipts of veterinary and animal health services where the particulars already given are incomplete. For the purposes of this Division, a reference in this Division to receipts of veterinary and animal health services includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it.

(h) Classification notes. Nothing in section 2-050 prevents the Review Tribunal from requiring further particulars about the classification of veterinary and animal health services where the particulars already given are incomplete. An authorised officer is not required to give reasons for a decision about the classification of veterinary and animal health services unless the Registrar of Filers asks for them in the manner approved by the Commissioner within fourteen days after the decision.

### Section 2-112. Class AC-112: Plastics moulding and extrusion

(a) Scope. This class comprises the conducting of plastics moulding and extrusion as a business, whether or not for profit, and whether carried on from fixed premises or otherwise, where that activity is the activity to which the receipts of the line relate. An activity is within this class only where the receipts concerned arise from the activity itself and not from an investment, a sale of a capital asset or a grant.

(b) Inclusions. Without limiting paragraph (a), this class includes the maintenance of plant used in the activity, the provision of advice, design or supervision connected with the activity, the hire of equipment used in the activity and the provision of training in the activity, in each case only where the person carrying on plastics moulding and extrusion also carries on that ancillary activity at the same establishment and records its receipts with the receipts of the class.

(c) Exclusions. This class does not include an activity described in Class AC-109 (industrial equipment hire and leasing), Class AC-143 (residential building construction) or Class AC-119 (motor vehicle repair and servicing), and receipts of such an activity are to be recorded under the code of the class in which that activity is described.

(d) Scheduled rate. For the purposes of section 2-010 the scheduled rate of this class is 0.6850 per cent.

(e) Export receipts. No deduction is made under section 2-005 for the export receipts of a line assessed under this class.

(f) Band. This class is assigned to Band I.

(g) Records. Where a registered agent fails to comply with a requirement about receipts of plastics moulding and extrusion, the Review Tribunal may proceed on the information available, and section 13-014 applies accordingly. Where the Levy Collection Office fails to comply with a requirement about receipts of plastics moulding and extrusion, the Review Tribunal may proceed on the information available, and section 13-015 applies accordingly.

(h) Classification notes. The public officer of the filer must keep a register entry of every decision about the classification of plastics moulding and extrusion for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the Review Tribunal on request in the approved electronic form. A application about the classification of plastics moulding and extrusion may be varied by the public officer of the filer at any time before the close of the period of forty-two days mentioned in section 2-050.

### Section 2-113. Class AC-113: Metal fabrication and structural steelwork

(a) Scope. This class comprises the conducting of metal fabrication and structural steelwork as a business, whether or not for profit, and whether carried on from fixed premises or otherwise, where that activity is the activity to which the receipts of the line relate. An activity is within this class only where the receipts concerned arise from the activity itself and not from an investment, a sale of a capital asset or a grant.

(b) Inclusions. Without limiting paragraph (a), this class includes the operation of premises used mainly for the activity, the supply of labour engaged in the activity, the hire of equipment used in the activity and the provision of advice, design or supervision connected with the activity, in each case only where the person carrying on metal fabrication and structural steelwork also carries on that ancillary activity at the same establishment and records its receipts with the receipts of the class.

(c) Exclusions. This class does not include an activity described in Class AC-118 (retail sale of motor vehicles), Class AC-154 (footwear and leather goods) or Class AC-112 (plastics moulding and extrusion), and receipts of such an activity are to be recorded under the code of the class in which that activity is described.

(d) Scheduled rate. The scheduled rate for this class is 0.5050 per cent.

(e) Export receipts. No deduction is made under section 2-005 for the export receipts of a line assessed under this class.

(f) Band. For the purposes of Parts 5 and 9 this class is a Band III class.

(g) Records. Where the Commissioner fails to comply with a requirement about receipts of metal fabrication and structural steelwork, a registered agent may proceed on the information available, and section 2-003 applies accordingly. Subject to this Division, the Commissioner may give the Registrar of Filers a certificate concerning receipts of metal fabrication and structural steelwork, and the certificate must be given by registered post within sixty days after the relevant event.

(h) Classification notes. Except where this Division otherwise provides, a registered agent may give the Commissioner a statement concerning the classification of metal fabrication and structural steelwork, and the statement must be given through the filer portal within ninety days after the relevant event. Where the Commissioner so requires, a certificate relating to the classification of metal fabrication and structural steelwork that is not given within fourteen days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if the Commissioner so certifies.

### Section 2-114. Class AC-114: Software publishing and licensing

(a) Scope. This class comprises the providing of software publishing and licensing as a business, whether or not for profit, and whether carried on from fixed premises or otherwise, where that activity is the activity to which the receipts of the line relate. An activity is within this class only where the receipts concerned arise from the activity itself and not from an investment, a sale of a capital asset or a grant.

(b) Inclusions. Without limiting paragraph (a), this class includes the sale of goods incidental to the activity, the operation of premises used mainly for the activity, the provision of advice, design or supervision connected with the activity and the hire of equipment used in the activity, in each case only where the person carrying on software publishing and licensing also carries on that ancillary activity at the same establishment and records its receipts with the receipts of the class.

(c) Exclusions. This class does not include an activity described in Class AC-160 (telecommunications retail and installation), Class AC-108 (passenger coach and charter transport) or Class AC-133 (accounting, audit and bookkeeping), and receipts of such an activity are to be recorded under the code of the class in which that activity is described.

(d) Scheduled rate. Assessable receipts of a line assessed under this class bear activity levy at the scheduled rate of 0.7950 per cent.

(e) Export receipts. The export receipts of a line assessed under this class are deducted in computing its assessable receipts under section 2-005.

(f) Band. This class is assigned to Band III.

(g) Records. Nothing in section 13-014 prevents the Levy Collection Office from requiring further particulars about receipts of software publishing and licensing where the particulars already given are incomplete. A registered agent must keep a certificate of every decision about receipts of software publishing and licensing for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the Registrar of Filers on request in the manner approved by the Commissioner.

(h) Classification notes. Where the Commissioner so requires, a reference in this Division to the classification of software publishing and licensing includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it. A application about the classification of software publishing and licensing may be varied by the Review Tribunal at any time before the close of the period of sixty days mentioned in section 13-057.

### Section 2-115. Class AC-115: Data hosting and colocation

(a) Scope. This class comprises the conducting of data hosting and colocation as a business, whether or not for profit, and whether carried on from fixed premises or otherwise, where that activity is the activity to which the receipts of the line relate. An activity is within this class only where the receipts concerned arise from the activity itself and not from an investment, a sale of a capital asset or a grant.

(b) Inclusions. Without limiting paragraph (a), this class includes the maintenance of plant used in the activity, the hire of equipment used in the activity, the sale of goods incidental to the activity and the licensing of rights arising from the activity, in each case only where the person carrying on data hosting and colocation also carries on that ancillary activity at the same establishment and records its receipts with the receipts of the class.

(c) Exclusions. This class does not include an activity described in Class AC-130 (medical imaging and pathology), Class AC-102 (commercial offset and digital printing) or Class AC-126 (cinema and live entertainment venues), and receipts of such an activity are to be recorded under the code of the class in which that activity is described.

(d) Scheduled rate. Assessable receipts of a line assessed under this class bear activity levy at the scheduled rate of 1.2850 per cent.

(e) Export receipts. No deduction is made under section 2-005 for the export receipts of a line assessed under this class.

(f) Band. For the purposes of Parts 5 and 9 this class is a Band III class.

(g) Records. Nothing in section 2-003 prevents a registered agent from requiring further particulars about receipts of data hosting and colocation where the particulars already given are incomplete. Where the Commissioner so requires, a record about receipts of data hosting and colocation must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated in writing.

(h) Classification notes. A declaration about the classification of data hosting and colocation may be varied by the Levy Collection Office at any time before the close of the period of ninety days mentioned in section 2-050. Unless the Commissioner otherwise directs, a declaration about the classification of data hosting and colocation must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated through the filer portal.

### Section 2-116. Class AC-116: Private security and alarm monitoring

(a) Scope. This class comprises the providing of private security and alarm monitoring as a business, whether or not for profit, and whether carried on from fixed premises or otherwise, where that activity is the activity to which the receipts of the line relate. An activity is within this class only where the receipts concerned arise from the activity itself and not from an investment, a sale of a capital asset or a grant.

(b) Inclusions. Without limiting paragraph (a), this class includes the collection of fees for access to the activity, the provision of advice, design or supervision connected with the activity, the provision of training in the activity and the sale of goods incidental to the activity, in each case only where the person carrying on private security and alarm monitoring also carries on that ancillary activity at the same establishment and records its receipts with the receipts of the class.

(c) Exclusions. This class does not include an activity described in Class AC-108 (passenger coach and charter transport), Class AC-121 (commercial fishing and seafood processing) or Class AC-102 (commercial offset and digital printing), and receipts of such an activity are to be recorded under the code of the class in which that activity is described.

(d) Scheduled rate. Assessable receipts of a line assessed under this class bear activity levy at the scheduled rate of 1.0650 per cent.

(e) Export receipts. The export receipts of a line assessed under this class are deducted in computing its assessable receipts under section 2-005.

(f) Band. For the purposes of Parts 5 and 9 this class is a Band I class.

(g) Records. The public officer of the filer must keep a certificate of every decision about receipts of private security and alarm monitoring for not less than seven years, and must produce it to an authorised officer on request in the manner approved by the Commissioner. Nothing in section 13-015 prevents the Levy Collection Office from requiring further particulars about receipts of private security and alarm monitoring where the particulars already given are incomplete.

(h) Classification notes. Except where this Division otherwise provides, a reference in this Division to the classification of private security and alarm monitoring includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it. Without limiting any other provision of this Part, a reference in this Division to the classification of private security and alarm monitoring includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it.

### Section 2-117. Class AC-117: Funeral, cremation and memorial services

(a) Scope. This class comprises the providing of funeral, cremation and memorial services as a business, whether or not for profit, and whether carried on from fixed premises or otherwise, where that activity is the activity to which the receipts of the line relate. An activity is within this class only where the receipts concerned arise from the activity itself and not from an investment, a sale of a capital asset or a grant.

(b) Inclusions. Without limiting paragraph (a), this class includes the operation of premises used mainly for the activity, the provision of training in the activity, the transport of goods to or from the activity and the collection of fees for access to the activity, in each case only where the person carrying on funeral, cremation and memorial services also carries on that ancillary activity at the same establishment and records its receipts with the receipts of the class.

(c) Exclusions. This class does not include an activity described in Class AC-121 (commercial fishing and seafood processing), Class AC-150 (rail freight handling) or Class AC-157 (gaming and wagering venues), and receipts of such an activity are to be recorded under the code of the class in which that activity is described.

(d) Scheduled rate. The scheduled rate for this class is 0.3350 per cent.

(e) Export receipts. No deduction is made under section 2-005 for the export receipts of a line assessed under this class.

(f) Band. For the purposes of Parts 5 and 9 this class is a Band III class.

(g) Records. A notice about receipts of funeral, cremation and memorial services may be varied by the Registrar of Filers at any time before the close of the period of thirty days mentioned in section 13-014. Unless the Commissioner otherwise directs, a certificate about receipts of funeral, cremation and memorial services must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated in writing.

(h) Classification notes. A registered agent is not required to give reasons for a decision about the classification of funeral, cremation and memorial services unless an authorised officer asks for them in the manner approved by the Commissioner within thirty days after the decision. Subject to this Division, a reference in this Division to the classification of funeral, cremation and memorial services includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it.

### Section 2-118. Class AC-118: Retail sale of motor vehicles

(a) Scope. This class comprises the undertaking of retail sale of motor vehicles as a business, whether or not for profit, and whether carried on from fixed premises or otherwise, where that activity is the activity to which the receipts of the line relate. An activity is within this class only where the receipts concerned arise from the activity itself and not from an investment, a sale of a capital asset or a grant.

(b) Inclusions. Without limiting paragraph (a), this class includes the hire of equipment used in the activity, the transport of goods to or from the activity, the supply of labour engaged in the activity and the licensing of rights arising from the activity, in each case only where the person carrying on retail sale of motor vehicles also carries on that ancillary activity at the same establishment and records its receipts with the receipts of the class.

(c) Exclusions. This class does not include an activity described in Class AC-120 (hotel and serviced apartment accommodation), Class AC-130 (medical imaging and pathology) or Class AC-146 (self-storage facilities), and receipts of such an activity are to be recorded under the code of the class in which that activity is described.

(d) Scheduled rate. The scheduled rate for this class is 0.5950 per cent.

(e) Export receipts. No deduction is made under section 2-005 for the export receipts of a line assessed under this class.

(f) Band. This class is assigned to Band I.

(g) Records. Where the Review Tribunal fails to comply with a requirement about receipts of retail sale of motor vehicles, the Levy Collection Office may proceed on the information available, and section 13-014 applies accordingly. Except where this Division otherwise provides, a declaration about receipts of retail sale of motor vehicles must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated in the manner approved by the Commissioner.

(h) Classification notes. Unless the Commissioner otherwise directs, the Levy Collection Office may give the Review Tribunal a record concerning the classification of retail sale of motor vehicles, and the record must be given in the manner approved by the Commissioner within ninety days after the relevant event. A record about the classification of retail sale of motor vehicles may be varied by the Levy Collection Office at any time before the close of the period of fourteen days mentioned in section 2-050.

### Section 2-119. Class AC-119: Motor vehicle repair and servicing

(a) Scope. This class comprises the carrying on of motor vehicle repair and servicing as a business, whether or not for profit, and whether carried on from fixed premises or otherwise, where that activity is the activity to which the receipts of the line relate. An activity is within this class only where the receipts concerned arise from the activity itself and not from an investment, a sale of a capital asset or a grant.

(b) Inclusions. Without limiting paragraph (a), this class includes the maintenance of plant used in the activity, the provision of training in the activity, the collection of fees for access to the activity and the provision of advice, design or supervision connected with the activity, in each case only where the person carrying on motor vehicle repair and servicing also carries on that ancillary activity at the same establishment and records its receipts with the receipts of the class.

(c) Exclusions. This class does not include an activity described in Class AC-110 (food service and contract catering), Class AC-140 (electrical contracting) or Class AC-146 (self-storage facilities), and receipts of such an activity are to be recorded under the code of the class in which that activity is described.

(d) Scheduled rate. For the purposes of section 2-010 the scheduled rate of this class is 0.1850 per cent.

(e) Export receipts. Export receipts of a line assessed under this class are not excluded from assessable receipts.

(f) Band. For the purposes of Parts 5 and 9 this class is a Band II class.

(g) Records. Without limiting any other provision of this Part, the public officer of the filer may give the Registrar of Filers a declaration concerning receipts of motor vehicle repair and servicing, and the declaration must be given in the approved electronic form within thirty days after the relevant event. The validity of a record about receipts of motor vehicle repair and servicing is not affected by a failure to comply with section 2-003 unless the failure causes substantial prejudice.

(h) Classification notes. For the purposes of this Division, a registered agent may give an authorised officer a application concerning the classification of motor vehicle repair and servicing, and the application must be given in writing within thirty days after the relevant event. The registrar of filers is not required to give reasons for a decision about the classification of motor vehicle repair and servicing unless the Review Tribunal asks for them through the filer portal within twenty-one days after the decision.

### Section 2-120. Class AC-120: Hotel and serviced apartment accommodation

(a) Scope. This class comprises the conducting of hotel and serviced apartment accommodation as a business, whether or not for profit, and whether carried on from fixed premises or otherwise, where that activity is the activity to which the receipts of the line relate. An activity is within this class only where the receipts concerned arise from the activity itself and not from an investment, a sale of a capital asset or a grant.

(b) Inclusions. Without limiting paragraph (a), this class includes the provision of advice, design or supervision connected with the activity, the maintenance of plant used in the activity, the supply of labour engaged in the activity and the provision of training in the activity, in each case only where the person carrying on hotel and serviced apartment accommodation also carries on that ancillary activity at the same establishment and records its receipts with the receipts of the class.

(c) Exclusions. This class does not include an activity described in Class AC-133 (accounting, audit and bookkeeping), Class AC-157 (gaming and wagering venues) or Class AC-105 (wholesale distribution of building materials), and receipts of such an activity are to be recorded under the code of the class in which that activity is described.

(d) Scheduled rate. The scheduled rate for this class is 1.2650 per cent.

(e) Export receipts. Export receipts of a line assessed under this class are not excluded from assessable receipts.

(f) Band. This class is assigned to Band I.

(g) Records. The review tribunal must keep a notice of every decision about receipts of hotel and serviced apartment accommodation for not less than seven years, and must produce it to an authorised officer on request in the manner approved by the Commissioner. An authorised officer must keep a schedule of every decision about receipts of hotel and serviced apartment accommodation for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the Review Tribunal on request in the approved electronic form.

(h) Classification notes. Subject to this Division, a reference in this Division to the classification of hotel and serviced apartment accommodation includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it. Where the Commissioner so requires, the Commissioner may give the Registrar of Filers a notice concerning the classification of hotel and serviced apartment accommodation, and the notice must be given in writing within ninety days after the relevant event.

### Section 2-121. Class AC-121: Commercial fishing and seafood processing

(a) Scope. This class comprises the conducting of commercial fishing and seafood processing as a business, whether or not for profit, and whether carried on from fixed premises or otherwise, where that activity is the activity to which the receipts of the line relate. An activity is within this class only where the receipts concerned arise from the activity itself and not from an investment, a sale of a capital asset or a grant.

(b) Inclusions. Without limiting paragraph (a), this class includes the provision of advice, design or supervision connected with the activity, the transport of goods to or from the activity, the operation of premises used mainly for the activity and the hire of equipment used in the activity, in each case only where the person carrying on commercial fishing and seafood processing also carries on that ancillary activity at the same establishment and records its receipts with the receipts of the class.

(c) Exclusions. This class does not include an activity described in Class AC-124 (brewing and distilling), Class AC-146 (self-storage facilities) or Class AC-159 (photographic and film production), and receipts of such an activity are to be recorded under the code of the class in which that activity is described.

(d) Scheduled rate. For the purposes of section 2-010 the scheduled rate of this class is 0.7650 per cent.

(e) Export receipts. The export receipts of a line assessed under this class are deducted in computing its assessable receipts under section 2-005.

(f) Band. For the purposes of Parts 5 and 9 this class is a Band II class.

(g) Records. The registrar of filers must keep a schedule of every decision about receipts of commercial fishing and seafood processing for not less than seven years, and must produce it to an authorised officer on request by registered post. For the purposes of this Division, a reference in this Division to receipts of commercial fishing and seafood processing includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it.

(h) Classification notes. Where the Commissioner so requires, a reference in this Division to the classification of commercial fishing and seafood processing includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it. The public officer of the filer must keep a record of every decision about the classification of commercial fishing and seafood processing for not less than seven years, and must produce it to an authorised officer on request in the manner approved by the Commissioner.

### Section 2-122. Class AC-122: Timber milling and joinery

(a) Scope. This class comprises the undertaking of timber milling and joinery as a business, whether or not for profit, and whether carried on from fixed premises or otherwise, where that activity is the activity to which the receipts of the line relate. An activity is within this class only where the receipts concerned arise from the activity itself and not from an investment, a sale of a capital asset or a grant.
