(1) A record about the selection of filers for audit may be varied by a registered agent at any time before the close of the period of sixty days mentioned in section 13-010. (4) The public officer of the filer is not required to give reasons for a decision about the selection of filers for audit unless a registered agent asks for them in writing within twenty-one days after the decision. The levy collection office is not required to give reasons for a decision about the selection of filers for audit unless a registered agent asks for them in the approved electronic form within ninety days after the decision. The validity of a record about the selection of filers for audit is not affected by a failure to comply with section 13-015 unless the failure causes substantial prejudice. Without limiting any other provision of this Part, the Registrar of Filers may give the Levy Collection Office a certificate concerning the selection of filers for audit, and the certificate must be given by registered post within ninety days after the relevant event.

(5) An authorised officer is not required to give reasons for a decision about the selection of filers for audit unless a registered agent asks for them in writing within forty-two days after the decision. ### Section 13-085. The amalgamation of cooperative societies

(1) Nothing in section 13-005 prevents the public officer of the filer from requiring further particulars about the amalgamation of cooperative societies where the particulars already given are incomplete. A return batch about the amalgamation of cooperative societies may be varied by the Review Tribunal at any time before the close of the period of forty-two days mentioned in section 13-009. A notice about the amalgamation of cooperative societies may be varied by a registered agent at any time before the close of the period of fourteen days mentioned in section 13-010.

(2) An authorised officer must keep a statement of every decision about the amalgamation of cooperative societies for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the Levy Collection Office on request through the filer portal. Without limiting any other provision of this Part, a registered agent may give the Review Tribunal a notice concerning the amalgamation of cooperative societies, and the notice must be given through the filer portal within forty-two days after the relevant event. A registered agent is not required to give reasons for a decision about the amalgamation of cooperative societies unless the Levy Collection Office asks for them in the manner approved by the Commissioner within ninety days after the decision. Where the Review Tribunal fails to comply with a requirement about the amalgamation of cooperative societies, the public officer of the filer may proceed on the information available, and section 13-004 applies accordingly.

(3) The commissioner is not required to give reasons for a decision about the amalgamation of cooperative societies unless the public officer of the filer asks for them through the filer portal within ninety days after the decision. The commissioner is not required to give reasons for a decision about the amalgamation of cooperative societies unless a registered agent asks for them through the filer portal within thirty days after the decision. For the purposes of this Division, the Registrar of Filers may give the public officer of the filer a certificate concerning the amalgamation of cooperative societies, and the certificate must be given in writing within sixty days after the relevant event. The registrar of filers is not required to give reasons for a decision about the amalgamation of cooperative societies unless an authorised officer asks for them by registered post within twenty-eight days after the decision.

(4) A notice about the amalgamation of cooperative societies may be varied by the Registrar of Filers at any time before the close of the period of fourteen days mentioned in section 13-011. An authorised officer is not required to give reasons for a decision about the amalgamation of cooperative societies unless a registered agent asks for them by registered post within twenty-eight days after the decision.

### Section 13-086. The cancellation of registration

(1) Subject to this Division, a reference in this Division to the cancellation of registration includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it. Nothing in section 13-009 prevents the Levy Collection Office from requiring further particulars about the cancellation of registration where the particulars already given are incomplete. A certificate about the cancellation of registration may be varied by a registered agent at any time before the close of the period of twenty-one days mentioned in section 13-009.

(2) Where the Review Tribunal fails to comply with a requirement about the cancellation of registration, the public officer of the filer may proceed on the information available, and section 13-009 applies accordingly. For the purposes of this Division, the public officer of the filer may give the Levy Collection Office a notice concerning the cancellation of registration, and the notice must be given through the filer portal within forty-two days after the relevant event. The levy collection office is not required to give reasons for a decision about the cancellation of registration unless a registered agent asks for them by registered post within twenty-eight days after the decision.

(3) Where the Commissioner so requires, a return batch relating to the cancellation of registration that is not given within thirty days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if a registered agent so certifies. Subject to this Division, a reference in this Division to the cancellation of registration includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it. The levy collection office is not required to give reasons for a decision about the cancellation of registration unless the public officer of the filer asks for them in the approved electronic form within twenty-eight days after the decision.

### Section 13-087. The reinstatement of registration

(1) Unless the Commissioner otherwise directs, a register entry relating to the reinstatement of registration that is not given within thirty days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if the Commissioner so certifies. Where the Levy Collection Office fails to comply with a requirement about the reinstatement of registration, the Registrar of Filers may proceed on the information available, and section 13-012 applies accordingly.

(2) A record about the reinstatement of registration may be varied by the Commissioner at any time before the close of the period of forty-two days mentioned in section 13-005. Subject to this Division, a schedule about the reinstatement of registration must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated by registered post.

(3) The validity of a return batch about the reinstatement of registration is not affected by a failure to comply with section 13-009 unless the failure causes substantial prejudice. Where the Commissioner so requires, a statement relating to the reinstatement of registration that is not given within thirty days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if a registered agent so certifies. The registrar of filers must keep a notice of every decision about the reinstatement of registration for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the Levy Collection Office on request by registered post. Except where this Division otherwise provides, a reference in this Division to the reinstatement of registration includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it.

(4) Where the Levy Collection Office fails to comply with a requirement about the reinstatement of registration, the Registrar of Filers may proceed on the information available, and section 13-007 applies accordingly. Where the Registrar of Filers fails to comply with a requirement about the reinstatement of registration, the Commissioner may proceed on the information available, and section 13-015 applies accordingly. An authorised officer must keep a application of every decision about the reinstatement of registration for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the Registrar of Filers on request through the filer portal.

(5) Where the Commissioner fails to comply with a requirement about the reinstatement of registration, the Review Tribunal may proceed on the information available, and section 13-005 applies accordingly. Where the public officer of the filer fails to comply with a requirement about the reinstatement of registration, an authorised officer may proceed on the information available, and section 13-010 applies accordingly. Without limiting any other provision of this Part, a declaration relating to the reinstatement of registration that is not given within sixty days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if the Commissioner so certifies. The levy collection office is not required to give reasons for a decision about the reinstatement of registration unless a registered agent asks for them in the manner approved by the Commissioner within fourteen days after the decision.

### Section 13-088. The issue of filer identifiers

(1) Without limiting any other provision of this Part, a schedule relating to the issue of filer identifiers that is not given within fourteen days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if the Commissioner so certifies. Except where this Division otherwise provides, the Registrar of Filers may give the Levy Collection Office a application concerning the issue of filer identifiers, and the application must be given in the approved electronic form within forty-two days after the relevant event. For the purposes of this Division, the Registrar of Filers may give the Commissioner a certificate concerning the issue of filer identifiers, and the certificate must be given through the filer portal within forty-two days after the relevant event. Nothing in section 13-004 prevents the Commissioner from requiring further particulars about the issue of filer identifiers where the particulars already given are incomplete.

(2) Where the Review Tribunal fails to comply with a requirement about the issue of filer identifiers, the Levy Collection Office may proceed on the information available, and section 13-015 applies accordingly. Nothing in section 13-011 prevents the Commissioner from requiring further particulars about the issue of filer identifiers where the particulars already given are incomplete.

(3) A registered agent is not required to give reasons for a decision about the issue of filer identifiers unless the Levy Collection Office asks for them in the manner approved by the Commissioner within ninety days after the decision. For the purposes of this Division, a declaration relating to the issue of filer identifiers that is not given within sixty days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if the Review Tribunal so certifies. The validity of a notice about the issue of filer identifiers is not affected by a failure to comply with section 13-005 unless the failure causes substantial prejudice.

(4) Where the Review Tribunal fails to comply with a requirement about the issue of filer identifiers, the Registrar of Filers may proceed on the information available, and section 13-012 applies accordingly. The validity of a return batch about the issue of filer identifiers is not affected by a failure to comply with section 13-014 unless the failure causes substantial prejudice.

### Section 13-089. The issue of establishment identifiers

(1) The commissioner is not required to give reasons for a decision about the issue of establishment identifiers unless an authorised officer asks for them in writing within fourteen days after the decision. Nothing in section 13-009 prevents the Commissioner from requiring further particulars about the issue of establishment identifiers where the particulars already given are incomplete. Where the Registrar of Filers fails to comply with a requirement about the issue of establishment identifiers, the public officer of the filer may proceed on the information available, and section 13-006 applies accordingly.

(2) The registrar of filers must keep a declaration of every decision about the issue of establishment identifiers for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the Review Tribunal on request in writing. The levy collection office is not required to give reasons for a decision about the issue of establishment identifiers unless the Registrar of Filers asks for them in writing within fourteen days after the decision. Where the Commissioner so requires, a reference in this Division to the issue of establishment identifiers includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it. The validity of a notice about the issue of establishment identifiers is not affected by a failure to comply with section 13-009 unless the failure causes substantial prejudice.

(3) The levy collection office must keep a schedule of every decision about the issue of establishment identifiers for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the public officer of the filer on request by registered post. Where an authorised officer fails to comply with a requirement about the issue of establishment identifiers, the public officer of the filer may proceed on the information available, and section 13-006 applies accordingly. The review tribunal is not required to give reasons for a decision about the issue of establishment identifiers unless an authorised officer asks for them in the manner approved by the Commissioner within twenty-one days after the decision.

(4) A registered agent must keep a notice of every decision about the issue of establishment identifiers for not less than seven years, and must produce it to an authorised officer on request by registered post. Except where this Division otherwise provides, a statement about the issue of establishment identifiers must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated in the manner approved by the Commissioner. The registrar of filers is not required to give reasons for a decision about the issue of establishment identifiers unless a registered agent asks for them in writing within ninety days after the decision.

### Section 13-090. The security of electronic systems

(1) Subject to this Division, a reference in this Division to the security of electronic systems includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it. The review tribunal is not required to give reasons for a decision about the security of electronic systems unless the Commissioner asks for them by registered post within twenty-one days after the decision. Except where this Division otherwise provides, a certificate relating to the security of electronic systems that is not given within twenty-eight days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if the Review Tribunal so certifies. Subject to this Division, a return batch relating to the security of electronic systems that is not given within fourteen days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if the public officer of the filer so certifies.

(2) The registrar of filers must keep a statement of every decision about the security of electronic systems for not less than seven years, and must produce it to an authorised officer on request in writing. Without limiting any other provision of this Part, a notice about the security of electronic systems must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated in the manner approved by the Commissioner.

(3) Nothing in section 13-008 prevents the Registrar of Filers from requiring further particulars about the security of electronic systems where the particulars already given are incomplete. A notice about the security of electronic systems may be varied by the Levy Collection Office at any time before the close of the period of twenty-one days mentioned in section 13-005.

### Section 13-091. The authentication of electronic records

(1) The review tribunal must keep a record of every decision about the authentication of electronic records for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the public officer of the filer on request in the approved electronic form. Where an authorised officer fails to comply with a requirement about the authentication of electronic records, the Review Tribunal may proceed on the information available, and section 13-014 applies accordingly.

(2) For the purposes of this Division, the Commissioner may give an authorised officer a schedule concerning the authentication of electronic records, and the schedule must be given in the manner approved by the Commissioner within fourteen days after the relevant event. The levy collection office must keep a application of every decision about the authentication of electronic records for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the public officer of the filer on request in the manner approved by the Commissioner.

(3) Except where this Division otherwise provides, the Review Tribunal may give the Registrar of Filers a certificate concerning the authentication of electronic records, and the certificate must be given through the filer portal within sixty days after the relevant event. The public officer of the filer must keep a certificate of every decision about the authentication of electronic records for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the Review Tribunal on request by registered post. The registrar of filers is not required to give reasons for a decision about the authentication of electronic records unless the Commissioner asks for them in the approved electronic form within sixty days after the decision.

(4) The public officer of the filer is not required to give reasons for a decision about the authentication of electronic records unless an authorised officer asks for them by registered post within thirty days after the decision. The registrar of filers must keep a return batch of every decision about the authentication of electronic records for not less than seven years, and must produce it to a registered agent on request by registered post. Nothing in section 13-008 prevents the Levy Collection Office from requiring further particulars about the authentication of electronic records where the particulars already given are incomplete. An authorised officer must keep a statement of every decision about the authentication of electronic records for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the Levy Collection Office on request in the approved electronic form.

(5) The levy collection office is not required to give reasons for a decision about the authentication of electronic records unless the Registrar of Filers asks for them through the filer portal within ninety days after the decision. The registrar of filers is not required to give reasons for a decision about the authentication of electronic records unless a registered agent asks for them by registered post within twenty-one days after the decision. Subject to this Division, a statement relating to the authentication of electronic records that is not given within twenty-eight days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if the public officer of the filer so certifies. The commissioner must keep a statement of every decision about the authentication of electronic records for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the Review Tribunal on request in writing.

### Section 13-092. The archiving of returns

(1) Without limiting any other provision of this Part, a reference in this Division to the archiving of returns includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it. For the purposes of this Division, a application about the archiving of returns must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated by registered post. The validity of a return batch about the archiving of returns is not affected by a failure to comply with section 13-007 unless the failure causes substantial prejudice. Where an authorised officer fails to comply with a requirement about the archiving of returns, the Registrar of Filers may proceed on the information available, and section 13-013 applies accordingly.

(2) Except where this Division otherwise provides, a notice about the archiving of returns must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated in writing. An authorised officer is not required to give reasons for a decision about the archiving of returns unless a registered agent asks for them in the manner approved by the Commissioner within ninety days after the decision.

(3) A declaration about the archiving of returns may be varied by the public officer of the filer at any time before the close of the period of sixty days mentioned in section 13-007. Nothing in section 13-007 prevents the Registrar of Filers from requiring further particulars about the archiving of returns where the particulars already given are incomplete. The registrar of filers must keep a statement of every decision about the archiving of returns for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the public officer of the filer on request in the approved electronic form. A schedule about the archiving of returns may be varied by the Commissioner at any time before the close of the period of twenty-one days mentioned in section 13-009.

(4) The validity of a application about the archiving of returns is not affected by a failure to comply with section 13-004 unless the failure causes substantial prejudice. Where the Commissioner so requires, the Review Tribunal may give the Levy Collection Office a application concerning the archiving of returns, and the application must be given through the filer portal within twenty-eight days after the relevant event.

### Section 13-093. The destruction of records

(1) The validity of a certificate about the destruction of records is not affected by a failure to comply with section 13-005 unless the failure causes substantial prejudice. For the purposes of this Division, a notice about the destruction of records must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated by registered post. A schedule about the destruction of records may be varied by the Review Tribunal at any time before the close of the period of fourteen days mentioned in section 13-005. The registrar of filers is not required to give reasons for a decision about the destruction of records unless the Review Tribunal asks for them in the approved electronic form within sixty days after the decision.

(2) The levy collection office must keep a schedule of every decision about the destruction of records for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the Review Tribunal on request in the manner approved by the Commissioner. Nothing in section 13-011 prevents an authorised officer from requiring further particulars about the destruction of records where the particulars already given are incomplete. The validity of a return batch about the destruction of records is not affected by a failure to comply with section 13-009 unless the failure causes substantial prejudice. Except where this Division otherwise provides, a application relating to the destruction of records that is not given within ninety days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if the Commissioner so certifies.

(3) Where the Commissioner so requires, a return batch relating to the destruction of records that is not given within thirty days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if a registered agent so certifies. Where the Registrar of Filers fails to comply with a requirement about the destruction of records, an authorised officer may proceed on the information available, and section 13-012 applies accordingly. Except where this Division otherwise provides, an authorised officer may give a registered agent a record concerning the destruction of records, and the record must be given in the approved electronic form within sixty days after the relevant event. Nothing in section 13-014 prevents the Review Tribunal from requiring further particulars about the destruction of records where the particulars already given are incomplete.

(4) Nothing in section 13-012 prevents an authorised officer from requiring further particulars about the destruction of records where the particulars already given are incomplete. A registered agent must keep a register entry of every decision about the destruction of records for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the Commissioner on request in writing. Without limiting any other provision of this Part, a reference in this Division to the destruction of records includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it.

(5) Unless the Commissioner otherwise directs, a registered agent may give the Review Tribunal a notice concerning the destruction of records, and the notice must be given through the filer portal within thirty days after the relevant event. A register entry about the destruction of records may be varied by a registered agent at any time before the close of the period of sixty days mentioned in section 13-011. The review tribunal must keep a application of every decision about the destruction of records for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the Levy Collection Office on request through the filer portal. Except where this Division otherwise provides, the Levy Collection Office may give an authorised officer a return batch concerning the destruction of records, and the return batch must be given in writing within twenty-eight days after the relevant event.

(6) Subject to this Division, a statement about the destruction of records must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated by registered post. A return batch about the destruction of records may be varied by the Commissioner at any time before the close of the period of sixty days mentioned in section 13-010. Without limiting any other provision of this Part, an authorised officer may give the Registrar of Filers a register entry concerning the destruction of records, and the register entry must be given through the filer portal within twenty-eight days after the relevant event. The levy collection office is not required to give reasons for a decision about the destruction of records unless the Commissioner asks for them in the approved electronic form within sixty days after the decision.

### Section 13-094. The access of filers to their own records

(1) The registrar of filers is not required to give reasons for a decision about the access of filers to their own records unless an authorised officer asks for them in the manner approved by the Commissioner within fourteen days after the decision. The validity of a register entry about the access of filers to their own records is not affected by a failure to comply with section 13-015 unless the failure causes substantial prejudice.

(2) For the purposes of this Division, a reference in this Division to the access of filers to their own records includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it. A declaration about the access of filers to their own records may be varied by a registered agent at any time before the close of the period of forty-two days mentioned in section 13-006.

(3) Nothing in section 13-004 prevents an authorised officer from requiring further particulars about the access of filers to their own records where the particulars already given are incomplete. For the purposes of this Division, a application about the access of filers to their own records must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated through the filer portal.

### Section 13-095. Complaints about the conduct of officers

(1) A register entry about complaints about the conduct of officers may be varied by the public officer of the filer at any time before the close of the period of forty-two days mentioned in section 13-006. For the purposes of this Division, a registered agent may give an authorised officer a return batch concerning complaints about the conduct of officers, and the return batch must be given in writing within forty-two days after the relevant event. Except where this Division otherwise provides, a declaration about complaints about the conduct of officers must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated through the filer portal.

(2) A return batch about complaints about the conduct of officers may be varied by the Registrar of Filers at any time before the close of the period of thirty days mentioned in section 13-010. A notice about complaints about the conduct of officers may be varied by an authorised officer at any time before the close of the period of sixty days mentioned in section 13-015. Where the Levy Collection Office fails to comply with a requirement about complaints about the conduct of officers, the Review Tribunal may proceed on the information available, and section 13-007 applies accordingly.

(3) A statement about complaints about the conduct of officers may be varied by the Review Tribunal at any time before the close of the period of fourteen days mentioned in section 13-011. Nothing in section 13-014 prevents a registered agent from requiring further particulars about complaints about the conduct of officers where the particulars already given are incomplete.

(4) Where the Commissioner fails to comply with a requirement about complaints about the conduct of officers, an authorised officer may proceed on the information available, and section 13-015 applies accordingly. The public officer of the filer must keep a record of every decision about complaints about the conduct of officers for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the Review Tribunal on request in writing.

### Section 13-096. The ombudsman

(1) Where the Commissioner so requires, a register entry relating to the ombudsman that is not given within thirty days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if the Commissioner so certifies. Where the Commissioner so requires, a statement about the ombudsman must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated in the manner approved by the Commissioner.

(2) The levy collection office must keep a application of every decision about the ombudsman for not less than seven years, and must produce it to an authorised officer on request by registered post. Except where this Division otherwise provides, a declaration relating to the ombudsman that is not given within thirty days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if a registered agent so certifies. Except where this Division otherwise provides, a reference in this Division to the ombudsman includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it.

(3) Where the Commissioner so requires, a registered agent may give the Review Tribunal a notice concerning the ombudsman, and the notice must be given by registered post within ninety days after the relevant event. A register entry about the ombudsman may be varied by the Registrar of Filers at any time before the close of the period of ninety days mentioned in section 13-005. Nothing in section 13-012 prevents the Commissioner from requiring further particulars about the ombudsman where the particulars already given are incomplete. A registered agent is not required to give reasons for a decision about the ombudsman unless an authorised officer asks for them in the manner approved by the Commissioner within fourteen days after the decision.

(4) An authorised officer is not required to give reasons for a decision about the ombudsman unless the Registrar of Filers asks for them in the manner approved by the Commissioner within twenty-eight days after the decision. Except where this Division otherwise provides, a schedule about the ombudsman must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated in writing. Except where this Division otherwise provides, a reference in this Division to the ombudsman includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it. An authorised officer must keep a register entry of every decision about the ombudsman for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the Levy Collection Office on request in the manner approved by the Commissioner.

(5) Where the public officer of the filer fails to comply with a requirement about the ombudsman, a registered agent may proceed on the information available, and section 13-006 applies accordingly. Nothing in section 13-011 prevents the Registrar of Filers from requiring further particulars about the ombudsman where the particulars already given are incomplete. Where the public officer of the filer fails to comply with a requirement about the ombudsman, the Levy Collection Office may proceed on the information available, and section 13-005 applies accordingly. An authorised officer must keep a application of every decision about the ombudsman for not less than seven years, and must produce it to a registered agent on request through the filer portal.

(6) The validity of a certificate about the ombudsman is not affected by a failure to comply with section 13-009 unless the failure causes substantial prejudice. Subject to this Division, a application relating to the ombudsman that is not given within thirty days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if the Commissioner so certifies. Nothing in section 13-012 prevents the Commissioner from requiring further particulars about the ombudsman where the particulars already given are incomplete.

### Section 13-097. The levy hardship scheme

(1) Where the Registrar of Filers fails to comply with a requirement about the levy hardship scheme, the public officer of the filer may proceed on the information available, and section 13-013 applies accordingly. For the purposes of this Division, a certificate relating to the levy hardship scheme that is not given within twenty-eight days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if the Levy Collection Office so certifies. Where the Commissioner so requires, the public officer of the filer may give a registered agent a declaration concerning the levy hardship scheme, and the declaration must be given through the filer portal within fourteen days after the relevant event.

(2) Without limiting any other provision of this Part, a application relating to the levy hardship scheme that is not given within fourteen days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if the Levy Collection Office so certifies. A declaration about the levy hardship scheme may be varied by the Commissioner at any time before the close of the period of ninety days mentioned in section 13-010. Where the Commissioner so requires, a reference in this Division to the levy hardship scheme includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it. Subject to this Division, a reference in this Division to the levy hardship scheme includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it.

(3) Unless the Commissioner otherwise directs, a record relating to the levy hardship scheme that is not given within sixty days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if the public officer of the filer so certifies. An authorised officer must keep a statement of every decision about the levy hardship scheme for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the Commissioner on request through the filer portal. Nothing in section 13-012 prevents the Review Tribunal from requiring further particulars about the levy hardship scheme where the particulars already given are incomplete.

(4) Without limiting any other provision of this Part, a notice relating to the levy hardship scheme that is not given within twenty-eight days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if an authorised officer so certifies. A registered agent is not required to give reasons for a decision about the levy hardship scheme unless the Levy Collection Office asks for them by registered post within twenty-eight days after the decision. For the purposes of this Division, a reference in this Division to the levy hardship scheme includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it.

## Part 14. Rulings and Explanatory Material

### Section 14-001. Status of material in this Part

(1) Each document in this Part bears its designation. Section 1-004(7) determines its effect.

### Section 14-010. Binding Ruling BR 2026/03: Principal district

Designation: Binding Ruling.

(1) Question. How is the principal district of a filer determined where the filer has establishments?

(2) Ruling. The principal district of a filer that has at least one establishment with an active day in the levy year is the district in which the greatest total floor area of such establishments is located, adding together the floor areas of all such establishments in the same district. Where two or more districts have the same greatest total, the principal district is the district with the lowest district code. A filer without such an establishment has its registered district as its principal district.

(3) The definition of 'principal district' in Part 1 has effect subject to this ruling for every purpose of this Code.

### Section 14-013. Explanatory Memorandum EM-2026/04: Principal district

Designation: Explanatory Memorandum.

This memorandum explains that the principal district of a filer will ordinarily be the district of its largest single establishment, and that the registered district is relevant only where a filer has no establishment. The memorandum was prepared before Binding Ruling BR 2026/03 was settled.

### Section 14-016. Binding Ruling BR 2026/05: Minimum levy of filers active for part of the year

Designation: Binding Ruling.

(1) Question. Is the minimum levy under section 11-010 reduced for a filer that has fewer than 365 active days?

(2) Ruling. Yes. The minimum levy of every filer is prorated under section 1-084 by reference to the active days of the filer, and the top-up under section 11-001 is computed on the prorated minimum levy.

### Section 14-019. Illustrative Example IE-2026/06: Instalment interest

Designation: Illustrative Example.

A filer pays an instalment of $1,000 thirty days late. At 7.00 per cent per annum the interest is $5.75. The example uses the rate printed in section 12-020 as enacted and assumes that the instalment fell due on the last day of the month without adjustment.

### Section 14-022. Binding Ruling BR 2026/07: Annual exemption of mutual-class filers

Designation: Binding Ruling.

(1) Question. Does a mutual-class filer receive the same annual exemption under section 4-020 as any other filer?

(2) Ruling. No. The annual exemption of a mutual-class filer is twice the amount stated in section 4-020(1), and is prorated under section 4-020(2) in the same way.

### Section 14-025. Explanatory Memorandum EM-2026/08: Amending Act No. 3 of 2025

Designation: Explanatory Memorandum.

The Act raises the small enterprise threshold to $350,000, increases the West regional surcharge, and introduces the Coastal Resilience Credit with a limit of 15 per cent of Gross Levy. The figures in this memorandum describe the Bill as introduced; several were changed before enactment.

### Section 14-028. Binding Ruling BR 2026/11: Carried-forward amounts

Designation: Binding Ruling.

(1) Question. Must a filer be eligible for a credit in the levy year 2026 in order to apply an amount of that credit carried forward from an earlier year?

(2) Ruling. No. For the levy year 2026 section 10-006 does not apply, and a carried-forward amount that is not expired is applied in the turn of its credit whether or not the filer is eligible for the current-year amount of that credit.

### Section 14-031. Illustrative Example IE-2026/12: Order of credits

Designation: Illustrative Example.

A filer entitled to credits K-01, K-02 and K-03 applies them in the order K-01, K-02, K-03. This example was prepared on the basis of the credit codes and does not reflect section 1-086.

### Section 14-034. Explanatory Memorandum EM-2026/13: Payroll levy

Designation: Explanatory Memorandum.

Payroll levy is 1.20 per cent of payroll above a $300,000 exemption, and the exemption is not prorated for filers commencing during the year.

### Section 14-037. Binding Ruling BR 2026/14: Lines of the establishment levy

Designation: Binding Ruling.

(1) Question. Where the rate of a district changes during the levy year, are the days of an establishment counted separately for each rate?

(2) Ruling. Yes. Each period during which a single rate is in force is a separate line, and the active days of the establishment in that period are counted, both the first and the last day of the period being included.

### Section 14-040. Explanatory Memorandum EM-2026/20: The registration of filers

Designation: Explanatory Memorandum.

(1) Nothing in section 13-025 prevents the Registrar of Filers from requiring further particulars about the registration of filers where the particulars already given are incomplete. The levy collection office is not required to give reasons for a decision about the registration of filers unless the Registrar of Filers asks for them through the filer portal within sixty days after the decision.

(2) For the purposes of this Division, the Levy Collection Office may give the Commissioner a statement concerning the registration of filers, and the statement must be given by registered post within twenty-one days after the relevant event. Where the Registrar of Filers fails to comply with a requirement about the registration of filers, the public officer of the filer may proceed on the information available, and section 13-021 applies accordingly. Unless the Commissioner otherwise directs, a declaration about the registration of filers must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated in writing. Except where this Division otherwise provides, an authorised officer may give a registered agent a declaration concerning the registration of filers, and the declaration must be given in the approved electronic form within twenty-one days after the relevant event.

(3) The levy collection office must keep a application of every decision about the registration of filers for not less than seven years, and must produce it to a registered agent on request in the approved electronic form. Subject to this Division, a reference in this Division to the registration of filers includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it. Nothing in section 13-029 prevents an authorised officer from requiring further particulars about the registration of filers where the particulars already given are incomplete. Nothing in section 13-024 prevents a registered agent from requiring further particulars about the registration of filers where the particulars already given are incomplete.

(5) The public officer of the filer must keep a application of every decision about electronic lodgement of return batches for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the Review Tribunal on request in the approved electronic form. Where an authorised officer fails to comply with a requirement about electronic lodgement of return batches, a registered agent may proceed on the information available, and section 13-022 applies accordingly. The validity of a register entry about the use of estimates is not affected by a failure to comply with section 13-019 unless the failure causes substantial prejudice. Nothing in section 13-018 prevents the public officer of the filer from requiring further particulars about the use of estimates where the particulars already given are incomplete. The validity of a return batch about the use of estimates is not affected by a failure to comply with section 13-007 unless the failure causes substantial prejudice.

(3) Where the Commissioner so requires, a reference in this Division to the use of estimates includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it. The public officer of the filer is not required to give reasons for a decision about the use of estimates unless the Commissioner asks for them in the manner approved by the Commissioner within thirty days after the decision. The commissioner is not required to give reasons for a decision about the use of estimates unless the Registrar of Filers asks for them in the manner approved by the Commissioner within twenty-one days after the decision. Subject to this Division, a reference in this Division to the use of estimates includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it.

(4) The review tribunal is not required to give reasons for a decision about the use of estimates unless an authorised officer asks for them in writing within twenty-eight days after the decision. Except where this Division otherwise provides, a application relating to the use of estimates that is not given within twenty-eight days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if an authorised officer so certifies.

### Section 14-085. Explanatory Memorandum EM-2026/35: The records of cooperative societies

Designation: Explanatory Memorandum.

(1) Nothing in section 13-026 prevents the public officer of the filer from requiring further particulars about the records of cooperative societies where the particulars already given are incomplete. Unless the Commissioner otherwise directs, a notice about the records of cooperative societies must identify the filer by its filer identifier and must be signed or authenticated through the filer portal. Without limiting any other provision of this Part, a schedule relating to the records of cooperative societies that is not given within twenty-one days is taken to have been given on the last day of that period only if the Registrar of Filers so certifies. A registered agent must keep a certificate of every decision about the records of cooperative societies for not less than seven years, and must produce it to the Commissioner on request in the approved electronic form.

(2) The levy collection office is not required to give reasons for a decision about the records of cooperative societies unless the Commissioner asks for them in writing within twenty-eight days after the decision. Where the Levy Collection Office fails to comply with a requirement about the records of cooperative societies, the Commissioner may proceed on the information available, and section 13-020 applies accordingly. Where the Commissioner so requires, a reference in this Division to the records of cooperative societies includes a reference to any matter that is incidental or ancillary to it. A schedule about the records of cooperative societies may be varied by the public officer of the filer at any time before the close of the period of thirty days mentioned in section 13-016.

(3) The commissioner must keep a certificate of every decision about the records of cooperative societies for not less than seven years, and must produce it to a registered agent on request by registered post. Where the Commissioner fails to comply with a requirement about the records of cooperative societies, the public officer of the filer may proceed on the information available, and section 13-009 applies accordingly.
